Entertainment Evidence

The Phoenician Resort and Spa · Scottsdale, AZ

Every captured guest review and online mention that references entertainment, shown verbatim with the entertainment language highlighted. 555 guest reviews and 149 web, social, and video mentions reference entertainment across the captured sources.

The case for an entertainment audit 9

The guests below name the entertainment program itself as the weak point — repetitive or prerecorded music, no nightlife, nothing to do at night, unfavorable comparisons to livelier resorts. Verbatim, with the entertainment language highlighted. Limited to reviews dated on or after the 2017-10-06 renovation, so these reflect the property as it is now, not pre-renovation history.

google · 2025-07-02 · 4/5view source
disappoint / activitieslack of / activities

A Beautiful Stay with Room for Improvement The Phoenician Resort is undeniably stunning, from the breathtaking mountain views near our hotel room to the beautifully landscaped grounds, multiple pool options, and towering palm trees that truly make the resort feel like a desert oasis. However, while the scenery impressed us, several aspects of our stay fell short, especially when compared to other top resorts in the Phoenix area. Dining & Amenities: One major concern was the limited dining hours. Most on-site restaurants closed by 5:00 PM, with The Tavern being the only option open until 8:00 PM. As out-of-town guests, this left us with little choice in the evenings aside from room service or ordering out. We expected more flexibility and variety in a luxury resort. Shops & Pool Experience: All shops on the property also closed early at 5:00 PM, and pool amenities, including the water slide, music, bar, and even the water fountains, were shut down around the same time. This was disappointing, as the pool is typically a hub of activity and relaxation, especially in the summer. Children's Activities: We were especially let down by the Funicians Kids Club, which was never open during our stay. This was a major reason we selected the Phoenician, and our children were incredibly disappointed by its unavailability. Summer Activities: We appreciated the advertised “Legendary Summers” theme, but there were no events scheduled during the weekdays only on the weekends. Other Phoenix-area resorts we’ve visited provide daily family-friendly activities all summer long, so the lack of weekday activities was a letdown. Overall, while the resort is visually impressive and offers a serene setting, we felt the guest experience didn’t fully match the high rating and reputation. With a few key improvements especially around extended hours, family activities, and consistent activites the Phoenician could offer a truly legendary experience to match its branding.

google · 2024-11-19 · 2/5view source
disappoint / showlimited / activities

I recently stayed at The Phoenician in Scottsdale with my wife and kids, and after reading all the glowing reviews, I just don’t understand the hype. It was fine, but I wouldn’t call it luxury by any means, nor exceptional, especially considering the price or the points required for a stay here. As a Gold Elite member with years of travel experience, I’ve come to recognize top-notch service and value, but this resort falls short in nearly every category. First impressions matter, and our check-in experience wasn’t great. We didn’t feel welcomed or even like we were at a luxury resort. The staff member who checked us in barely explained anything about the property—just handed me a map and a booklet and sent us on our way. The main pool area was closed for maintenance, and the kids' splash park wasn’t working when we tried to use it—not once, but twice. The only other pool available for kids was cold! Honestly, no one else was in it because it felt unheated, and after talking to a few guests, they agreed it was too cold. We also didn’t see any pool attendants around to ask about life jackets or pool toys, or anything that might make the experience better if the pool was heated. The kids' club or play area was also closed on Monday, so there was literally nothing for our kids to do for the first couple of days of our stay. Fortunately, we had a larger unit with a hot tub and a golf cart to get around, which helped salvage the experience—without that, there wouldn’t have been much to do. Speaking of the hot tub, not good. After using it on Sunday, the next day all of us had rashes or skin breakouts. We suspect it was from the excessive chlorine, as there was an unusually thick layer of foam and bubbles in the tub. Our 5-year-old, 1.5-year-old, and both adults all had reactions. Room 7704 itself was disappointing, especially for the price and points required. It’s showing its age—there were missing pieces of flooring near the dining table (my wife actually hurt her toe), cracks and dents in the walls, and the doors don’t close properly. The whole place just felt a bit run-down. I certainly wouldn’t pay thousands to stay here. The wifi also didn’t work for our entire stay which wasn’t ideal at all. I had a couple of meetings Monday and no wifi. Costing me money to use data. We called down to have someone come up and check it out Sunday but nobody came for almost 2 hours. Called back to inform them nobody came by, kids had to go to sleep. They were going to update the ticket and have someone come by Monday. Nobody came, no follow up, nothing. Just terrible service. We also ordered food from the Tavern one night, and it was mediocre at best—not worth the price at all. Save your money and eat elsewhere. As a Gold Elite member, I expected better treatment, especially with pools being closed and limited activities for kids on certain days of the week. We were hoping for an upgrade to a unit with a pool, which is supposed to happen as a gold elite member (enhanced room upgrade benefit) but despite some of the other residences being vacant both nights, they didn’t offer us anything. They were more focused on charging extra for upgrades than making sure we had a better experience, even though we were loyal members with kids and limited options at the resort. Honestly, I regret spending 240,000 points to stay here. This resort was a major disappointment overall, and I’ll definitely be looking elsewhere next time. It was a huge waste of both time and points. I encourage others looking to spend their hard earned dollars or points to look elsewhere. For those of you who love this place, glad you enjoyed it and had a better experience. I know it’s hard to please everyone and it’ll rarely happen but we felt overall, it simply underwhelming and not worth it.

We found this very expensive and not even worth considering.

tripadvisor · 2019-02-28 · 4/5view source
just sit / evening

The Khyber Resort & Spa- Luxurious And The Best Option In Gulmarg Rooms It is a 85-room resort with beautiful views of the valley and the Himalayas. Wooden ceilings, luxurious interiors and spacious rooms are some of the wonderful features of this resort. Our room had a balcony with majestic views of the Himalayas. While the morning and the evenings were chilly, with the temperatures dropping to almost 0 degrees, the days are lovely and we would step out or even sit with a book in the balcony. Cottages Apart from the rooms they also have an option of the cottages with a private hideaway. Fitness centre They a reasonable size gym with well appointed machines, dumbbells and other accessories. There are separate sauna and steam facilities as well as jacuzzis. Again the views from here make winter workouts interesting. Swimming Pool The one feature that is covered in almost all the magazines is their heated indoor swimming overlooking the snow laden coniferous forest. It’s a luxury you understand when you are in a cold, freezing atmosphere. Kids play area- IGLOO This was well equipped but at the basement and we tried going there twice but it was deserted so we skipped the same. They also have a games room if you want to catch a quick round of snooker or billiards. The Den They have the concept of the Den which is where you can watch movies of your choice but we never got the time to check this one out. Restaurants Cloves Most of our meals were eaten here. The breakfast has an amazing lay and for dinner and lunch they have a buffet and an ala carte option. I thought the food was pretty decent. If you are a non-vegetarian then their Kashmiri thali is a must-try though you might need two people to finish it off. Nouf Nouf has to be booked in advance and in our usual hurried trip style we never ended up doing the same but it is supposed to have stunning views from every direction. Freshly grilled meats, cooked just the way you like on traditional coal-fired barbecues. Nouf is ideal for a cozy dinner by the candlelight. Definitely on my list for the next time. Chaikash Think of it as their coffee shop. It is just next to their lobby and perfect place to lounge and have tea or Kahwa. Their Kahwa is absolutely delicious and for us it became an evening routine to just sit their with our books and chat and indulge in some couple time here. Do not forget to create a snowman and indulge in some snow fights while you are surrounded OTHER ACTIVITIES Trekking and Fishing Treks are closed in winters but if you head here in a slightly bearable weather the the trek to Khilanmarg Valley to see spring flowers is supposed to be enchanting. If you are an adventure seeker then you can trek to the serene Alpather Lake. For fishing enthusiasts Ningle Nallah and the Ferozepur Nallah are streams famous for trout fishing.

google · 2026-05-14 · 5/5view source

We had the pleasure of staying at the The Phoenician last month and thoroughly enjoyed the experience. The resort itself is aesthetically beautiful, with an impressive range of activities and spaces for both families and adults. Multiple pools, several restaurants, recreational options, and quiet nooks throughout the property make it a destination where nearly everyone can find a place to relax. What stood out most, however, was the service, particularly at check-in. Christian noticed that we shared a somewhat uncommon last name and took a few extra moments to genuinely get to know us. He made the arrival process effortless, but what happened later truly surprised us. When we returned to our room that evening, we found a heartfelt handwritten welcome note along with special snacks and a thoughtfully prepared margarita cocktail ensemble. That level of personalization is increasingly rare today, especially in an era where staffing shortages often leave guests feeling rushed through transactional interactions. Experiences like this are a reminder that service is personal and meaningful, and that organizations capable of delivering it consistently likely invest well in their people and culture. The rooms were well-appointed, the beds comfortable, and the bathrooms spacious and functional. As someone who admittedly notices details like water pressure, I can report that it was more than adequate. The on-site restaurants offered a nice variety of options, and the servers throughout the resort clearly understood what genuine hospitality looks like. The Tavern in particular was lively and enjoyable, with good live music and excellent service. A couple of practical notes for future guests: reservations are highly recommended for the restaurants. The seating process can feel a bit cumbersome, with multiple hand-offs even during slower periods. Additionally, poolside service could be more attentive at times, as we occasionally needed to flag staff down. That said, those issues were minor compared to the overall experience. While five stars may feel slightly generous given the price point and a few operational inefficiencies, on a 1–10 scale this resort is a very solid 9/10 and absolutely worthy of consideration for anyone visiting Scottsdale.

Nothing about this hotel is luxury. 4 star at best. We arrived to our interconnecting rooms and walked straight back out. In what can only be compared to a motel block, the carpet was taped down with black masking tape, stains over furniture, general signs of years of wear and tear were hugely obvious. Even our balcony had some sort of juice stain that hadn’t been cleaned from the previous guests. We went back to reception to tell them we were changing hotels when they suddenly found us interconnecting rooms in the main hotel (previously unavailable). Whilst still a far stretch from what we would consider luxury - especially for close to $1000 per night per room - these were better than our previous option. Douglas agreed to modify the rate too - thank goodness because we’d have been sick paying what we’d originally committed to. However the disappointment didn’t end there. Discarded glasses and rubbish decorate communal areas. Staircases and outdoor areas haven’t had a thorough sweep or wipe down for what looks like months. Pool loungers are very uncomfortable with thin basic towels. There is a queue for breakfast everyday and you’re unable to make reservations. Free coffee machines and milk canisters are often found empty. Asked for an ice coffee at the pool but apparently this isn’t on offer and I have to go into one of the restaurants to see if they might serve it - this would be totally acceptable for a hotel that doesn’t claim to be 5 star luxury. Staff are friendly enough, but most don’t go over and above. I can only assume lacking a manager who is experienced in what 5 star luxury actually means. It appears other recent reviews emulate our experience and I only wish we had studied these before making our reservation. Kids were happy enough with tennis and pool areas. But unless this hotel has a huge overhaul, its increasingly negative reputation will get the better of it.

google · 2025-07-02 · 4/5view source

A Beautiful Stay with Room for Improvement The Phoenician Resort is undeniably stunning, from the breathtaking mountain views near our hotel room to the beautifully landscaped grounds, multiple pool options, and towering palm trees that truly make the resort feel like a desert oasis. However, while the scenery impressed us, several aspects of our stay fell short, especially when compared to other top resorts in the Phoenix area. Dining & Amenities: One major concern was the limited dining hours. Most on-site restaurants closed by 5:00 PM, with The Tavern being the only option open until 8:00 PM. As out-of-town guests, this left us with little choice in the evenings aside from room service or ordering out. We expected more flexibility and variety in a luxury resort. Shops & Pool Experience: All shops on the property also closed early at 5:00 PM, and pool amenities, including the water slide, music, bar, and even the water fountains, were shut down around the same time. This was disappointing, as the pool is typically a hub of activity and relaxation, especially in the summer. Children's Activities: We were especially let down by the Funicians Kids Club, which was never open during our stay. This was a major reason we selected the Phoenician, and our children were incredibly disappointed by its unavailability. Summer Activities: We appreciated the advertised “Legendary Summers” theme, but there were no events scheduled during the weekdays only on the weekends. Other Phoenix-area resorts we’ve visited provide daily family-friendly activities all summer long, so the lack of weekday activities was a letdown. Overall, while the resort is visually impressive and offers a serene setting, we felt the guest experience didn’t fully match the high rating and reputation. With a few key improvements especially around extended hours, family activities, and consistent activites the Phoenician could offer a truly legendary experience to match its branding.

The Phoenician, a Luxury Collection Resort in Scottsdale, offers an enjoyable and upscale experience overall. The facilities are impressive, with a beautifully maintained spa and a gym that is top-notch. The on-site restaurants are well-kept, providing a pleasant dining atmosphere. While the resort is clearly making efforts to maintain its charm, some areas could benefit from further updates to match the high standards associated with its reputation. Nevertheless, The Phoenician remains a good choice for a relaxing getaway in Scottsdale, especially if you value amenities and serene surroundings.

google · 2024-11-19 · 2/5view source

I recently stayed at The Phoenician in Scottsdale with my wife and kids, and after reading all the glowing reviews, I just don’t understand the hype. It was fine, but I wouldn’t call it luxury by any means, nor exceptional, especially considering the price or the points required for a stay here. As a Gold Elite member with years of travel experience, I’ve come to recognize top-notch service and value, but this resort falls short in nearly every category. First impressions matter, and our check-in experience wasn’t great. We didn’t feel welcomed or even like we were at a luxury resort. The staff member who checked us in barely explained anything about the property—just handed me a map and a booklet and sent us on our way. The main pool area was closed for maintenance, and the kids' splash park wasn’t working when we tried to use it—not once, but twice. The only other pool available for kids was cold! Honestly, no one else was in it because it felt unheated, and after talking to a few guests, they agreed it was too cold. We also didn’t see any pool attendants around to ask about life jackets or pool toys, or anything that might make the experience better if the pool was heated. The kids' club or play area was also closed on Monday, so there was literally nothing for our kids to do for the first couple of days of our stay. Fortunately, we had a larger unit with a hot tub and a golf cart to get around, which helped salvage the experience—without that, there wouldn’t have been much to do. Speaking of the hot tub, not good. After using it on Sunday, the next day all of us had rashes or skin breakouts. We suspect it was from the excessive chlorine, as there was an unusually thick layer of foam and bubbles in the tub. Our 5-year-old, 1.5-year-old, and both adults all had reactions. Room 7704 itself was disappointing, especially for the price and points required. It’s showing its age—there were missing pieces of flooring near the dining table (my wife actually hurt her toe), cracks and dents in the walls, and the doors don’t close properly. The whole place just felt a bit run-down. I certainly wouldn’t pay thousands to stay here. The wifi also didn’t work for our entire stay which wasn’t ideal at all. I had a couple of meetings Monday and no wifi. Costing me money to use data. We called down to have someone come up and check it out Sunday but nobody came for almost 2 hours. Called back to inform them nobody came by, kids had to go to sleep. They were going to update the ticket and have someone come by Monday. Nobody came, no follow up, nothing. Just terrible service. We also ordered food from the Tavern one night, and it was mediocre at best—not worth the price at all. Save your money and eat elsewhere. As a Gold Elite member, I expected better treatment, especially with pools being closed and limited activities for kids on certain days of the week. We were hoping for an upgrade to a unit with a pool, which is supposed to happen as a gold elite member (enhanced room upgrade benefit) but despite some of the other residences being vacant both nights, they didn’t offer us anything. They were more focused on charging extra for upgrades than making sure we had a better experience, even though we were loyal members with kids and limited options at the resort. Honestly, I regret spending 240,000 points to stay here. This resort was a major disappointment overall, and I’ll definitely be looking elsewhere next time. It was a huge waste of both time and points. I encourage others looking to spend their hard earned dollars or points to look elsewhere. For those of you who love this place, glad you enjoyed it and had a better experience. I know it’s hard to please everyone and it’ll rarely happen but we felt overall, it simply underwhelming and not worth it.

Live instrumental music is notable and adds to the authentic feel . Room service is very quick . Caution- never leave property without a jacket and an umbrella.  Always negotiate with outsourced facilities. have a great holiday

This review has nothing to do with the room quality or amenities or facilities, those are all quite nice and frankly should be for the nightly rate you are paying. This review ties directly to the staff and their lack of care for their guests. We were there for a weekend with family and friends attending a wedding. On the day of the wedding my wife went to the pool for one hour by herself. She got her towels and set her things down on a chair. She then received a phone call that she took and spoke for 15 minutes next to the pool with her back turned to the chair. At this time someone grabbed all of her things including her purse and sandals. This pool probably had 20 guests at the pool at this time, not a lot of activity. All the staff had the same lethargic response, “I am sorry mam, there is nothing we can do.” Now let me be crystal clear, we are not ascribing blame directly to the hotel. They certainly cannot be responsible for all guests' belongings at all times and my wife made a costly error in judgment, although an unconscious one, she never should have turned her back to her items. We immediately shut down credit cards but saw that the culprit already tried to charge $1,500 at Neiman Marcus just down the street so now we had validation that this was no accident or misplaced items. One gentleman who worked at the front desk (I wish I had his name) even had the nerve to tell us, “Things get stolen from the pool all the time” - If that statement is true, maybe an examination of security protocols might be a priority. Rather than attend our wedding we spent the reception filling out a police report. We knew the chances of recovery were slim and the financial loss was significant (>$500) but not catastrophic. The point is there is an absolute dearth of security at this resort. I can’t list all the shortcomings but here are a few: There are no cameras anywhere on the three pool decks (at least that is what we were told), All gates to the pools remain wide open at all times. I have stayed at resorts who charge half as much as the phonecian and they still require a room key to access the pools. The parking lot entry attendant showed no enforcement as far as which cars were allowed on property. Here is the kicker. 7 hours after the purse was first stolen another charge was attempted on our canceled credit card BACK ON THE PHOENICIAN property. We thought this was our chance, how many establishments are even open at 10 PM to accept payment on the property? We alerted hotel management immediately and again the blank stares, the nonchalance, complete and utter deflection, “I am sorry, we can’t track that.” How easy would it have been upon our initial claim to tell their staff “If you run a credit card that is denied then alert security immediately.” With today’s tech and ubiquitous communication that is such an easy catch. You would think at $700/night there might be a modicum of effort or assistance for their guests. Moral of the Story: Don’t take anything valuable to the pool and Don’t rely on the staff. Their customer service extends as far as reading a script, over and over again. They are sorry, but there is nothing they can do, ahem, willing to do. I don’t mean to slight you directly Ben, you could be highly proficient at your role and I know this lack of attention to our case is not orchestrated by you. The frustration is with your bosses who are merely applying damage control. It is a strategy as old as time, deflect and neglect. Placate the upset client with feckless apologies in hopes they just go away. You allude to hints of action, “steps are being taken“ . . . Please feel free to contact us with what exactly these are. Luxury retreat at Khyber We stayed two nights at khyber. While you check in, you can sit at the Chaikash lounge which has an amazing Himalayan view and you can see the mountains and the beautiful trees. Rooms - we had premier balcony gulmarg view room, we really liked sitting in the balcony. The rooms are really luxurious and grand and the bathroom is huge Amenities- The views from the heated swimming pool are amazing. They have water googles if you forget, It's open from 9 to 9 so there's enough time to go visit it. I also used the steam room, which was really nice. I didn't use sauna or the hot tub but they have both of these. The spa looked really good as well but I didn't have time to use it. The igloo room, has ping pong and caram and some video games. It's nice but they made it in the basement. For the property this size, It would be nice to have it somewhere upstairs with windows and view. Food - For food, there are two restaurants. There's an Italian restaurant where we did not eat and the main restaurant which has breakfast and dinner buffet. I think they serve lunch as well but we didn't do lunch. The spread just huge. Fresh fruits and everything you can think of like pancakes, waffles, dosas, uttapams, parathas, all sorts of Indian stuff. South Indian breakfast is just amazing. They make you delicious cappuccinos on demand. What stands out the most is the staff at the restaurant. Firdaus and Rayees really were the good care of us. The dinner buffet is also a huge spread. It's practically impossible to even try everything that they have on the spread. The only complaint I have with dinner is their desserts. They have a huge selection but nothing else appealing to me or my family. So I think they can up their dessert game a little bit. But overall, the food was delicious. From the hotel you can walk to the gondola. We enjoyed our stay at Khyber and highly recommend to anyone else who would like to stay there. The lobby is huge and there are so many different settings sections which where you can sit and enjoy. And there is a musician who plays Santur and he is amazing. The music is so calm and soothing as well as entertaining at the same time. We enjoyed listening to him.

The one thing I would like to point out is that the hotel has a steep climb from the gondola station.. the gondola station is actually quite close so that is another advantage of staying here. So coming back to the steep climb, it would be nice if the hotel had a buggy kind of service for that. If they do have one, it was not mentioned to us. If my parents were with me, they would not have been able to walk up that road to the hotel. All in all, I would give it a 5 star. We loved it and we thank everyone who made out stay memorable

tripadvisor · 2022-05-31 · 5/5view source

Luxury with an extra touch of love and care We booked the Himalayan view deluxe rooms that come with a balcony sitout with brilliant views of the snow capped Apharwat peak and other ranges around. It's difficult to even imagine a better located property. It's a couple of mins walk from the prime attraction - the Gondola (cable car ropeway) that takes one close to the top of the Apharwat peak - and then a short snowy trek to get to the top. The Khilanmarg horse riding point surrounded by the characteristic conifers of Kashmir is a few mins walk too. Rooms and bathrooms are well appointed and decently sized, though not lavish. But what caught my attention most is the level of attention to detail in service. When we asked to be shifted to the upper floor from the originally assigned room on the lower floor corner - the request was accomodated with a smile with no hassle. I'm more used to dark, dirty looks from even the best of properties - when asking for anything they haven't offered upfront. The attitude of "yes, with pleasure", was apparent across all service staff from the very helpful concierge, restaurant staff, housekeeping and even the security guards. Rare to find an uniformly friendly, helpful band of staff - especially when they were very clearly running full house. The local kashmiri fare served a la care at their main restaurant Clove deserves a special mention, along with the excellent deli and bakery Chaikash. Their selection of bakery surprisingly rivaled some of the best I've sampled in Europe, and hardly ever if at all anywhere in India. Compliments to the chef Hira Singh (insta @chefhira). His fruit gateaux and butter croissants were particularly amazing. The rest of their Indian food leaves much to desire though. And the dinner buffet is a total disaster - or at least it was on the only evening we opted for it. The breakfast buffet is excellent though. Rest of the meals for us were all a la carte. A special call out to Firdaus at Cloves who took care of us during most of our meals both a la carte and buffet. And to Rahul who served us at Chaikash. But even perfect isn't ever perfectly perfect. Here are somethings that I thought could be better. - A travel desk even if it were outsourced to help with the rides, sightseeing etc. They do actually have a bevy of cab drivers, atv and horseride hire folks almost always present within the premises. A chat with the security person gets you what you need. But it's puzzling why it isn't better organized with a desk - Chaikash shuts by 6.30-7 PM. It's by far the best located with views of the peaks as well as beautifully set with lounge seats. While their sheesha lounge at the other end of the lobby opens when Chaikash shuts. It seems like converting Chaikash to accommodate the same services may have been a superior experience. I'm sure they have good reasons to do what they do. But it's not apparent. - Room cleaning is the only service that was surprisingly shoddy. Under the toilet and on tables were a hurried job with stains and streaks still visible post cleaning.

tripadvisor · 2022-03-23 · 5/5view source

Heavenly Khyber Staying at Khyber Himalayan Resort and Spa- an award winning resort, nestled 8825ft above the sea level in the Pirpanjal range of Himalaya- truly an experience of life time. It will mesmerise anyone once you enter in it- what a interior! A very well maintained star property with exquisite wooden work of authentic Kashmiri craftsmanship, lavishly designed room, very spacious with balcony and all modern amenities… luxury bathroom with bathtub, separate shower, toilet and changing area and A snowy view outside to indulge yourself into the world of true luxury. But the best of this property is it’s location.. what a view of Apharwat Himalaya, snowy slopes and forests of pine and deodar.. specially from the Himalayan view room… it will justify expense( approx 42k per night) which is really very high as per Indian standards. The entire property is centrally heated which will feel you very comfortable even in peak winter with minus temperature outside. The pool is also heated and the pool area has just extraordinary view of outside snowy slopes and mountains. We have stayed three nights in March 2022 and it was just an exceptional experience. The staffs are very courteous and helpful. However I felt few drawbacks too.. 1. Very high cost of food and buffet which may make you uncomfortable if you are planning for long stay like three nights. 2. Limited and average breakfast menu.. not justified as per rate. We have stayed many luxury hotels across India like Taj, J W, ITC group etc who are much ahead in terms of variety and cost effectiveness of buffet 3. Loccitane Spa- our first experience of L’Occitane spa was at J W Mussoorie which was just heavenly- with that expectation with took service at Khyber too.. but here our experience was little different.. firstly it is more costly as per the quality of service. Second they provide only male to male and female to female therapists.. so if you are taking couple service, your spouse must feel comfortable to take service in presence of a male therapist in a single room. Thirdly the lounge of spa is a common area for Gym and pool too, and specially in evening it’s over crowded with people of all age group. So if you wish to relax and enjoy some special moments before taking therapy, it’s not possible.( I’ve attached a pics of the chaos too!) 3. They almost never receive any phone calls.. but reply readily on email. Overall it’s a heavenly resort for a lifetime experience specially in winter months but definitely with a very high expenditure.

tripadvisor · 2020-02-20 · 4/5view source

Great location, good food, decent service. Travelled to Gulmarg in 1st week of January 2020. The intention was to enjoy snow as much as we can since there is none in New Delhi. I had booked 3 nights stay at Khyber from Makemytrip in november but as i got near to January i was worried if flights are cancelled due to bad weather what will happen to my hotel booking. But all went well and we reached Kashmir-heaven on Earth. I had arranged my transfer from Srinagar airport to Khyber and back with the hotel. It is a little expensive(INR 3500 each way) but they take care of everything then. As soon as i landed there was a person from hotel waiting in the luggage belt area. Got luggage and moved towards Gulmarg. The car sent was a 4x4 scorpio which surely is required to drive n the snow from Tangmarg-Gulmarg. Till Tangmarg the roads are clean and a normal car works but not from Tangmarg-Gulmarg.It was constantly snowing and there was a lot of snow on the road which made it slippery as well. On way to Gulmarg the driver assisted in renting the boots required for walking in snow from Tangmarg as normal shoes won't work in 5ft or more of snow.Always bargain everywhere in Kashmir. They ask for 3 times the normal prices for everything. On reaching the hotel was given welcome drink kashmiri Kahva-great taste. After the check-in formalities was shown way to my room which was what i booked a luxury balcony room with Gulmarg view. I would surely recommend everyone to book a balcony room to enjoy snow. Though you can hardly sit outside for long but having a balcony surely has its charm. The problem i faced before reaching Kashmir was i tried to connect with hotel several times and a lot of times the reservation person does not pick up the phone. The property is built with taste and i believe a lot of effort. The resort is centrally heated which is surely required.There is gym,heated pool , jacuzzi ,steam ,sauna in the hotel. The hotel also has a kids play area but it is next to nothing. I believe the snow is the best thing to play here for kids. The gym was decent with good views and working machines. Room: The room was spacious with central heating. Had a separate shower and a bath tub. Enough space for luggage , had a locker in the almirah , tea coffee, water sufficient in the room. The property is running old and they are not refurbishing things like the sofa in the room was in a very bad shape. It had lost all the cushioning and was dirty as well. No one cleaned balcony in 3 days and when i requested that there is too much snow in the balcony then they sent someone to clean it. The room has a desk but you cannot work on it. Not a good design to put a laptop and work. Spa/swimming pool: On the website it was mentioned that the pool is under renovation but it was operational when i reached hotel. Now the hotel sells the swim suits etc at exorbitant prices. the pool surely has the best views of snow clad mountains. The spa is not the best. The steam room does not work properly and someone needs to check that. There was hardly an attendant in the spa area. The property is minutes from the famous Gondola ride which will take you the mountains to enjoy the beauty of Kashmir. Though there are people at the resort who would want to be your guide for a fee but i recommend to enjoy yourself. Just walk down the road and take left and you will be at the ticket counter for Gondola. The guide / car hiring / sledging etc all are quoted 3 times by the people outside the resort. So bargain for everything.One of the days i just walked from the resort to the Gulmarg main market and there was no problem except people troubling you to hire sledges or car or take guide. Food: The food was good on all occasions here. 2 out of 3 nights we had the buffet as the manager offered us 1+1 deal on the buffet. Prices for a-la-carte are too steep. Breakfast was decent ,loved the south Indian dosa that they prepare. The hotel does not serve alcohol but you are welcome to get your own and drink in their shish lounge. They do serve shish and some snacks there. Phones : Were not working in Gulmarg. I had vodafone which did not have any signal in Gulmarg. Reliance & Airtel i believe work. Last word: The hotel is not the best 5 star property i have stayed in but surely it a commendable job done at such height. The prices they are charging are a little on higher side but i thing to maintain this property in Kashmir surely is a big task.

tripadvisor · 2019-04-07 · 5/5view source

Luxury Resort in Gulmarg The impeccable colour combinations, minimalist aesthetics, and understated furnishings do not detract from the beauty of the place and makes it feel impeccably elegant and timeless. Besides the Kashmir style elements, the place has gilded ceilings and stunning wall artifacts, which are a feast for the eyes. Blending the tradition of Kashmir heritage with contemporary sophistication, the palatial building of The Khyber Resort has a stylish ambience that creates an ultra-luxurious vibe. The spectacular lobby has an impressive reception desk, concierge and a sprawling multi cuisine restaurant that throws open the spectacular scenic view of the now capped mountains. The lobby definitely makes a great first impression with its cozy interior and impeccable designing that is somewhat similar to the beauty of the paradise land. The staff is extremely friendly & helpful, and will go the extra mile to make your stay as memorable as possible. From the lobby, the plush carpeting takes you up to the luxury guestrooms and plush suites. Rooms The design in all rooms brilliantly combines contemporary style with local accents. The rooms at Khyber are spacious, luxuriously decorated, and most of them have wide balconies to take in the views. During our 2 night visit to the hotel, we stayed in a luxury balcony room, which overlooked the stunning picturesque beauty of the mountains. It featured a magnificent carpeting, a large walk-in closet, a plush king bed, a wide sofa cum bed, tea and coffee sachets and flat-screen TV. The rooms are also really quiet and well insulated. The beds are spacious, and linens are of superior quality. There’s nothing quite like a luxury hotel that not only has great interiors, offers excellent service, and has crisp bed-linen you want to curl up in. The marble bathroom had a wide mirror, sink, a separate rain shower and a deep-soaking bathtub. One of the best things about hotel stays are soaking in a tub filled with warm water. The Bathrooms feature luxurious bath amenities. The big windows with views offering a spectacular view of the mountains make you want to stay in the tub forever. The Presidential suite @ The Khyber has contemporary furnishings that complement authentic silk wall coverings, chandeliers, wood paneling and ceilings painted in soft hues. Bedrooms are heavenly, temperature controlled and have all the luxury amenities. The suites are also equipped with kitchen, servant’s room and a balcony which offers a nice view of the mountains. Since Khyber is a luxury and expensive resort, one can get some discounts by using coupons from online travel sites like Goibibo. Dining Khyber has an exquisite multi-cuisine restaurant called Cloves that has authentic Kashmir cuisine and continental dishes that are served with warmth and hospitality. The Restaurant offers amazing views that you can soak in while dining. The best thing is that you can also order for room service in your own room as per your own convenience. The hotel also has a popular lounge called Chaikash at the farthest end that serves the most quintessentially coffee, tea and kahwa. This place is one of the most desirable and enjoyable place in the hotel to sit and relax with a cup of hot coffee in your hands. The picturesque lounge opens up to an impressive view of the skyline with the majestic mountains in the background. Calabash, their sheesha lounge, has fine carpets and seating, designed to invigorate lounging for hours on end and floor-length windows that look out to forests of fir and the Himalayas. This is indeed, a cosy nook, which gives you the much needed warmth after a chilly outing in the snow. The chefs are diligent and ensure the freshest food is served with a touch of innovation. We specially loved the continental treats like the grilled fish, lobster and crab cakes. I would highly recommend the salmon to be tried. Also not to missed are their delectable buffet spreads that speak in volumes about the enormous variety and freshness. The breakfast buffed spreads are the most amazing ones and comprise of fresh juices, detox shots, salads, yoghurts, dips, variants of breads along with continental breakfast like sausages, hams and eggs. The Southern delicacies include dosas, idlis, vadas and uttapams. The Indoor heated pool and Gym The Indoor heated pool, which is surrounded by a phenomenal 360 degree view of the snow capped mountains look amazing and make you feel relaxed while soaking in the warm water of the pool. Heated to a comfortable temperature, you can take a leisurely dip or do laps to stay in shape. The gym is well equipped with the latest machines and helps you in exercising with the assistance of the in-house instructor. The steam, sauna and jacuzzi add to the pampering and should definitely be experienced. I had booked for a luxury Aromatherapy massage during my stay at their luxurious L’Occitane Spa. I decided to head down early and take a dip in the pool – which is an experience of being totally immersed in soft lights – and later relax in the Jacuzzi followed by steam and sauna.

tripadvisor · 2019-03-07 · 5/5view source

Absolutely fabulous! Insanely brilliant service and the slopes and snow are so good! I stayed here for 7 nights from 02Feb to 09Feb. We were 2 people, who came to snowboard. Airport pickup: we had asked the hotel for an airport pick and drop. This was very smooth. There was a guy right at the baggage belt, with our names on a placard and he took us out to the car, in the parking lot. The car driver was nice and friendly and we stopped in the way for a quick bite. Check in: Was smooth. We sat in the lounge while it was done. A bit weird, but I was asked for the invoice for the booking, which I clearly didn’t have (who does). Anycase, that wasn’t a hindrance. Checkin went smooth. Rooms: there are rooms with our without balcony. We took one with a balcony. Ideally, in winters, avoid a balcony room. Also, the balcony rooms come with Gulmarg view and mountain view - the mountain view is gorgeous! (Perhaps for summer). Restaurant: the restaurant was good and the quality of food and the service was quite nice. There is a variety of food. We did the dinner buffet one night, which was a bit disappointing - 6/10, I’d say. Breakfast was very well laid and great quality. Bar : the hotel doesn’t serve/sell alcohol but you’re welcome to bring your own booze and sit in the sisha lounge and drink. This worked out quite comfortably. There is service there and you get everything you need. A SHOUTOUT to Firdous, at the Shisha lounge. His service was exemplary. Made us feel welcome and service was very personalised. Ski/snowboard: the gondola is about a 400m walk from the hotel but it’s a steep climb back to the hotel. Unlike all good ski resorts, here there is no shuttle service. I asked the management and was told that the local taxi drivers don’t allow hotel cars (to maintain their monopoly). The local taxi guys charge 500rupees for this 500m trip. Possibly the only downside. Gondola : beautiful. Well maintained. Hotel pool and gym : The gym is very good, well equipped and quite scenic. The pool is very scenic, but for winter months, I think it’s a bit cold. I was hoping it to be like an onsen (like in Japan) or a hottub(in the states/Europe), but its much cooler which made me refrain from going into it. Internet: the internet of the hotel is quite good, considering how remote the hotel is. All in all - 9/10 (possible because of shuttle service). Will come back.

A 5 Star Hotel that's gives India it's own slice of Switzerland and more !! I have been attempting to book a room here over the Christmas period for the past 2 years now and I always end up reacting a little late by when all rooms get lapped up. This time not to be outdone I ensured I was miles ahead and had a lovely "Gulmarg View with Balcony room" waiting for me. I had high expectations of the hotel and rightly so, it's website and promotional video are one of the finest I have ever seen and being a seasoned traveller was a few percentile sceptical that it might be as one would expect "dressed up" for the promotion material. I went about the hotel with a fine tooth comb looking for reasons to give them a thumbs down and boy was it tough. The pictures of the rooms / bathrooms etc are an exact representation of the hotel and this is a rarity. On check in we were greeted by the lovely Ms Khalida (who upon reading this will remember I promised to give a fair unbiased review on check out :) There was lovely Cava a hot local beverage that was served to all of us and the check in procedure was flawless. I did arrive at about 4 and probably missed the rush since check in is at 2 and I recommend this during the peak season if your not the wait patiently type. We had stayed for 3 nights over the Christmas period and you can't get more festive than that. They had a Santa Clause prancing around giving out sweets to the kids on demand through the day, a nice little touch I must say. The Breakfast buffet had a mix of Indian and continental fare enough for everyone to indulge in as per ones palate. I do recommend as I did with the check in, however the reverse in this case show up early as there is a bit of a wait if you come in late in order to get a table. Dinner was a buffet as well though I do recommend going over the Ala carte menu for a more robust choice especially if your looking to sample various types of ethnic non vegetarian fare. They do have a few on the buffet but if you want the real kickers be prepared to order from the menu. I have seen a few reviews where people complain that the restaurant is expensive, my answer is Khyber is not exactly a budget property, if you can afford the room rates there should he no reason to penny pinch elsewhere. There are a host of guides standing outside the foyer and on day 1 I was not sure if they were there to dive deep into my pockets, then again you can negotiate terms but the service is if I may say so as promised. Do not hesitate to use there services even for a few hundred more it's worth it in terms of convenience. The Spa run by Loccitane as all their Spas was exemplary and the pool the finest I have been to in my life in terms of the over all experience. The Jaccuzi was HOT and had 8 seats, and not a lacklustre mildy warm experience that some hotels have for the sake of saying they offer a Jaccuzi. I have read other reviews where one was complaining about the size of the Sauna well it perhaps could have been slightly larger but I'm considering every time I went there was just 1 or 2 other people it did not matter. I also did read a review a few days ago of someone who had a terrible experience thats what one would call the joker in the pack, I also did have a slight issue of a chocked shower drain which was intended to immediately. No hotel is without it's imperfections therefore one must in view of the over all situation allow somethings to pass. I have picked holes in the finest properties on earth that being said were all human. The housekeeping team deserves special mention especially Muzzamil who tended to every need from extra towels to a turn down service without a word. Every time I walked the passages on cue Every single time irrespective of who the staff member was I was greeted throughout my stay with a smile and a Good morning /, evening, it's these little things that go a long way. I did travel by my 8 and 5 year old and if any one needs suggestions on the property with children do write in. My children had a BALL at the Igloo which is basically a den in the basement with a lot of entertainment in tow. I have returned and have recommended the property to my friends and Khyber has already had a few reservations confirmed in view of my excellent experience. I thank everyone at Khyber and will definitely be back dinner than later.

tripadvisor · 2018-12-02 · 5/5view source

Location location location (and the POOL) What a perfect location. Breathtaking views no matter which window you look out of. The Good - Location: Stunning views and amazing proximity from the Gondola and ATVs. Perfectly located - Heated pool: Definitely the highlight of Khyber. Perfect temperature indoors with the snow clad Himalayas and beautiful tall pines - Spa: A must do, hard to explain the experience, just go for it - In-Room dining: the food we ordered in-room (italian, Indian and snacks) were excellent. - Room: bedroom and bathroom are an ideal size. The bed is huge and comfortable. - Clean: very well maintained hotel. great housekeeping - Service: courteous, helpful and flexible staff like it should be in a 5 star. We lost one of our jackets at the airport. It was tracked, the hotel taxi arranged for it to be collected and delivered to our room (at no charge). During the dinner buffet, we wanted a white sauce pasta for our 3 year old, that wasn't on the menu, but they made it and didn't charge extra. We asked for a tour of the hotel, and they spent a good hour showing us every nook and cranny. Could Improve - Buffet: While the breakfast and dinner buffets are good, they could be better. Eg. in breakfast, would be good to have more options of cold cuts, assortment of cheese, granola / cereals and non-veg. - Food prices: Be ready to go light on your wallets - Response time: We made a couple of requests where it look a bit longer that we expected for the staff to arrive - like a personal tour of the property (which was amazing, must do), and luggage collection at check out (its like they didn't want us to leave ;) ). - Cupboard: is a little tiny. Make use of the drawers in the room - Bathroom hanging: between the towels, robes and wet swimsuits, you'll have insufficient hooks for hanging - Kids entertainment: while every kids channel is available, the play area could do with a few more options to keep kids engaged for longer. - Internet: speed is a bit slow, mobile 4G (at least Airtel) is way faster. Will get better results using your hot-spot. Travel tips - Get a guide: Go to the Gondola with a guide especially if you have a child. He will help manoeuver and carry the kid at slippery places. Its a big help. - Negotiate: Even if its 'fixed price' for activities like skiing, snow-biking etc. Your guide will help you pull down the price by 50% - Sledge: not worth it, stick to skiing. snow biking and the 13KM ATV ride - Boots and coat: rent a set for your trip up the gondola ride. If its snowing, make sure you have them for your ATV ride as well. Whenever you stop and jump into the snow, you'll need them.

tripadvisor · 2018-08-28 · 5/5view source

85 key resort is framed at every vantage point by nature at its most spectacular. A world away from the ordinary, this 85 key resort is framed at every vantage point by nature at its most spectacular. The world’s highest ski lift, the Gulmarg Gondola – which takes you to Kongdoori Mountain, is only a short walk away. Meadows and forests that have enchanted travellers for centuries wait to be explored by trekking enthusiasts and undying romantics. Clad in locally sourced pine and slate, the resort is built in a style true to the architectural heritage of Kashmir. In every way the indoors mirror the beauty of the outdoors. Speciality restaurants, a Sheesha lounge, a well-appointed spa and a heated swimming pool are amongst the necessary luxuries in place. Add to these other thoughtful additions like our children’s club, a mini theatre and a dedicated Activities Concierge and you will find The Khyber is a resort at par with the best in the world.

Almost all food in the breakfast are local foods. Not a big deal for many I guess but since we have kids, it was a bit of a problem for us. And at this price range aroud 400usd/night, food variety should be much better. But overall, it was truly an amazing place and we had a wonderful stay.

tripadvisor · 2018-03-05 · 5/5view source

A great family hotel... We booked the Khyber and ever since I was full of skepticism. I had seen various reviews here on trip advisor regarding poor food or service etc etc. Hence I went (along with my husband and 2 daughters- 8yo and 3yo) with an open mind. I must say we were in for a lovely surprise. The moment we entered the property (which was very warm - literally - thanks to the massive heating facilities) , we were given a more than warm welcome by Mr. Sohail. This made us feel very welcome and absolutely at home. We had booked a 1 bedroom cottage which Is a little away from the reception (but connected to the main building via the corridors). The room was very warm and toasty! It was snowing outside and we could move around the entire hotel in shorts! Rooms- We booked the one bedroom cottage. It had a living room, a bathroom and a small maid’s room in the ground floor with a small verandah opening into the gardens. The top floor was a very spacious bedroom with a huge balcony with breadth taking views. The bathroom was very clean, well equipped and heated! Food- All the food was above average. We tried south Indian, Chinese, Italian and Indian cuisine. The breakfast spread was endless with live South Indian and egg counters. With freezing temperatures and such hostile weather, we must appreciate the fact that each and every ingredient that goes into preparation of the food is brought from the plains ( Srinagar mostly). And I think they are doing a fabulous job serving delicious and piping hot meals. - The heated pool is a must visit but it does get very crowded in peak season - the pool area has lovely heated jacuzzis, steam and sauna too - ‘chaikash’ serves free Kashmiri kawah (very delicious 😋) along with some lovely bakery preparations and snacks - the spa is very good and must visit - all postpaid mobile networks work in entire Gulmarg including the level 1 gondola place - liquor is not served in the hotel ( infant it’s born served anywhere in Gulmarg). However, you may carry your own drinks. - beware of the guides and touts waiting outside the hotel. They are all cheats. Please do a thorough research on trip advisor and book in advance. - we did skiing and loved ‘discover Gulmarg adventures’ run by Irfan and his very talented team. - the entire staff from the hotel waiters ( Junaid in particular ), housekeeping staff , restaurant managers etc were all very very warm and considerate. Only few pointers that I hope the hotel looks into - -We saw lots of families with kids , but the property could be a little more kid friendly. The play area needs urgent upkeep. Some more activities need to be introduced. Overall a super family vacation - we shall definitely visit again.

tripadvisor · 2017-09-25 · 5/5view source

Into the Himalayas – The Khyber Surrounded with snow covered mountains and conifers, Gulmarg, situated in the western Himalayas, is described as the centre of winter sports in India. Due to its breath-taking panoramic beauty this place has been the shooting spot for many Hindi movies. Must mention the ‘Bobby Hut’ here, a scene from the famous Bollywood movie ‘Bobby’ was shot in a hut at this place which is now called as the ‘Bobby Hut’. Normally, people visit this beautiful place for enjoying the winter activities like Skiing, Tobogganing, Biking (Snow Scooters), etc. Interesting Fact: Gulmarg Gondola is claimed to be the world’s second highest cable car service. Gulmerg has got many hotels and family resorts. Our stay at The Khyber Himalayan Resort and Spa proved to be one of the best of its kind. Firstly they greet you introduce you to the Khawa, a regional tea. A cup of Khawa is enough to keep you warm throughout the day. They have maintained an impressive garden which is a nice place to sit and witness the charming atmosphere in night. Staff is quiet friendly and helpful, guides you to your reserved rooms and helps you transfer your luggage. Eat & Drink Eating at Cloves, multi cuisine resto at Khyber is an amazing experience. The arrangement and the lighting add up to the pleasing ambience of the hotel. The food offered according to one person is amazing and enough to fill your stomach. Chaikash offers soothing and refreshing beverages while the Terrace Lounge is a nicely done al fresco dining with elegant views of surrounding area. Their Sheesha lounge ‘Calabash’ having big glass windows out looking the landscaped hills and forests, and Nauf filled with sweet fragrance of pines, are a good place to sit and enjoy the visit with traditional delicious barbeque dishes. Stay The Luxury Balcony room was nicely done with all modern amenities. High Speed Wireless Internet is easily accessible and does not have any connectivity issues. A big LED was placed right in front of the King size bed. A tea/coffee maker, some Cold Drinks and high quality nuts and chocolates and some fresh fruits were also available in the room for free of cost. The Bathroom had a sunken tub which was quite big, shower area and dressing room. It is equipped with high quality toiletries. The best part was the beautiful view of the snow caped mountains that came from the balcony and the bathroom as well. At night the atmosphere got even more magical. Beautiful Lamps light up and the moonlight falling on the snow made it look even more scenic. Play Besides a pool they have a fairly updated gymnasium and a separate section IGLOO for children (which feature many indoor games to play and enjoy). For Adults they had activities like skiing, trekking, RIDES. You can spend time at the world’s highest golf court. They also had a snooker room. Also for movie lovers they had THE DEN having the latest Bollywood and Hollywood releases. In summers they host activities like ATV Rides, gondola rides, mountain biking, rock climbing, Pony- Horse riding and jeep safaris. More Information Hotel: The Khyber Himalayan Resort & Spa Location: The Khyber Himalayan Resort & Spa, Gulmarg 193403, Kashmir (J&K), India Phone: +91 1954254666 Accommodation: 5 Star

Either that or pay 4000 for a taxi to the airport. When you stay at a luxury hotel, you don't expect to ever have to worry about these things, yet when something as basic as transportation cannot be provided, it's a real shame. Will not come here again.

tripadvisor · 2016-02-05 · 5/5view source

Truly, the best property in Gulmarg If you are traveling to Gulmarg and you want to stay in the best resort there, think no further. Our group of 12 (incl 6 kids) stayed at Khyber for two nights early January. The resort is right in the middle of Himalayas and in a picture perfect location. You can take those Kodak picture moments from any point in the resort. The interiors and exteriors are just splendid. The resort has a nice restaurant (clove), a coffee shop, a cigar lounge and a spa. We picked the regular deluxe room with extra bed in each room. Kids above 5 will be charged extra. Our package included breakfast and internet. The resort is less than 500 meters from the Gondola and a 10min walking distance from the Skiing area (beginners). The hotel premises were spotless and professionally managed. Breakfast and Dinner buffets at Clove were pretty good. The main course of the dinner buffet needs to be updated. I enjoyed the continental appetizers and hors d'oeuvres. Very little choices for non-vegetarian items in the main course. The hotel does not have a liquor license and it was disappointing. The cigar lounge is an interesting set up. You can smoke Cigars and Hookah but not cigarettes. You can bring your own liquor and the hotel will serve the rest of the stuff (snacks, sodas etc.) We enjoyed our time there. Some tips for fellow travelers: - It costs Rs. 300 to go from the hotel to the ski area or the Gondola (one way). And Hotel will not be able to arrange this for you. We were told that the cab services in Gulmarg are controlled by a small group. We struggled quite a bit to get a car when needed. There are couple of guys hanging out in the arrival area. They will not only try to get you a car but also try to sell you a general guide, tickets to gondola, tickets to ski slope, guide to ski slope etc. - And if you do not go with those additional services, you will not get your cab when you need it. This is border line blackmail. I hated this so much that we walked the 10min when we could. It is much harder to walk back to the Resort because of the steep incline of the road. - Best option is to make a deal with the car that drove you to the hotel from Tangmarg. - You will also be pressured by many people to hire a guide. We had at least 20 people that approached us during the 10 min walk from resort to Gondola. They asked anywhere from Rs.600 - Rs.2000. They will tell you many stories and try to scare you as well. We did not take a guide.I suggest that you pick one (can negotiate for Rs. 300) for two reasons. One is that you will not be approached by anyone else. The second reason is that the guide can discretely talk to the security guys in phase 1 of Gondola and extend the time you can spend there by a few house. - Buying tickets for the Gondola is quite easy. Once you are in the phase 1 area, you are required to return in one hour. - The published rates for snow mobile rides in Gondola are also exaggerated. We negotiated ride to PK1 for Rs.1000/person (published rate was Rs. 2000) - If you are looking for a guide + ski equipment, you will have to negotiate quite a bit as well. We were quoted anywhere from 2000 to 4000/ person for this. We did not make a decision until we reached the ski area. There we were able to negotiate a guide + ski equipment + lessons + skiing for 3 hours for Rs. 1000/person. That also included a cab ride back to the resort.

Definitely the best in Gulmarg or rather in whole of Jammu & Kashmir ! The resort is marvellous with its decor, ambience, location, and people. Location is the best bit. We travelled in a large group of Young Indians (Yi) - around 80 of us (yeah) !!! And had the resort to us. The rooms & suites were brilliant - each overlooking the Himalayan mountains or the Gulmarg village, both picturesque. The balconies in the rooms were just superb. The staff took great care. The managers and their team members were all brilliant. especially Mr. Mallik Zaffar. Although the food was quite good, the vegetarian options may be improved. The Kashmiri Tea - Kahwa - is served every day and is brilliant. The L'Occitane Spa is wonderful, though only same-gender therapists allowed. The temperature-controlled indoor swimming pool overlooking the valley is superb, however, it had some mosaic tiles coming off when we went and needed some repairs. There are various other entertainment options, but we didn't get to try many as we had lots to do with the group. Our arrangements were made all the more easy & brilliant because our complete tour's booking & stay arrangements were made by J&K's best travel agency - Shiraz Travels, Srinagar. We had a brilliant time going on one of world's highest ropeways - the Gulmarg Gondola. And we also enjoyed teeing off at one of world's highest golf courses too - the Gulmarg Golf Club !

tripadvisor · 2015-08-28 · 2/5view source

Discourteous Staff! I went to Khyber for afternoon tea. My plan was to return and stay at the hotel with my family in Dec 2015, during snow season. The infrastructure and decor of this hotel deserves a 5 star! Everything looks splendid. However, like mentioned in many of the other reviews, the behaviour of the staff really falls short of expectations. There are no clear instructions written about which areas/lounges are only for residents and what are their opening-closing timings. So we went to lounges, without being stopped by anyone (security or staff), only to be told very rudely later that we weren't suppose to do so. I understand that all hotels have their own rules, but if they don't let the guests know, how are we to guess? I have stayed in many hotels across different countries in the last 20 years. However, I haven't seen such discourteous staff before. If it wasn't for my friend, I don't think I would have stayed for my cup of tea (Kashmir khawa priced at INR 300 a cup). In hindsight, I shouldn't have. We were served without a smile. We paid with our credit cards, and there too the cashier was impolite. The only friendly person was the doorman at the main entrance. I don't think I want to come back to stay in a hotel that charges like INR 20,000 for a night, and yet treats its guests like it's doing us a favour!

tripadvisor · 2015-06-02 · 4/5view source

A destination in its own right There are those people who come to Gulmarg & then there are those who come to the Khyber. The Khyber has truly become a destination in its own right. Gulmarg was crying out for a property like this - top end rooms, very high end villas, good food & a great spa. The rooms are large, well appointed & comfortable. All the mod-cons are at your finger tips - temperature control, in room coffee/tea making making facilities, satellite TV, wifi (with slightly iffy speed & signal strength). The bathrooms are nice with a separate shower stall & a tub by the window. The tubs are perfect for a romantic soak as you watch the moon rise over the mountains. Room tip - ask for a room facing the spa because it's a better view though the rooms on the other side aren't shabby either. The hotel offers a multi cuisine restaurant, with both a buffet & a menu to choose from. The breakfast is a star meal with a live egg station and fabulous South Indian dosa-idli breakfasts. There is a tea lounge with a wide range of teas & coffees. If you are trying to kick the caffeine habit try the rose tea, it's amazing. A hookah or sheesha lounge is available tans is a nice place to spend the evening chatting & reliving the days experiences. Point to note - the hotel doesn't sell alcohol but will be happy to serve you your own if you bring it with you. The spa is a revelation with a lovely heated pool. Heaven is watching the snow come down as you lounge around in the pool. There is a gym with some basic free weights, a multi gym and some cardio equipment. I haven't tried the treatment rooms but it's a La Occitane spa so it must be great. So whether it's a honeymoon, a family holiday or a skiing trip this is a great place to stay.

If you are in Gulmarg Khyber is the place to stay.

tripadvisor · 2015-05-10 · 3/5view source

overhyped resort in the hands of unprofessional managers Khyber is a good property given its location and decent size. There aren't better option in and around Gulmarg either. However, it does lack the quality levels of other bigger players like Taj Vivanta and Lalit in Srinagar. The worst experience I had was about its non-transparent policy. We had booked our rooms online through hotel about 45 days in advance and we were offered Rs. 21,000 for their premier category on EP which we found was being offered at Rs.19,000 by the hotel. When we inquired about this, one of the managers at the reception suggested we must book through a travel agent in order to get the discount. The other said the hotel changes rates daily. They had no justification for the variation and were only trying to avoid the conversation. The desk manager was unfriendly and uncooperative. Moreover, considering the tariff the rooms were too small and stinking. For someone who does not want to go hiking/skiing or do any outdoor activities there is no point in staying at Gulmarg and/or Khyber. If one is only interested in experiencing the Gondola ride and visit the highest point, it is better to make a day trip from Srinagar which has better hotels and packages than Khyber for sure. Both Vivanta and Lalit are a lot better options than Khyber. It would be criminal to spend more than Vivanta at Khyber. Even while visiting the level 1 & 2 by Gondola, be careful about the full on cheating by the ski taxies (They transfer you to a short distance 400/500 mtrs on ski) which demand absurd amounts like Rs. 4000 per person. It is not worth spending more than Rs. 400 (four hundred). They try hard to scare you by talking about the adversities of the place, but one can easily walk down this distance. Please do not spend more than Rs. 500 on ski transfers.

tripadvisor · 2015-04-02 · 5/5view source

A deserving Luxury for Gulmarg Visited Khyber for 4nights during end of march. The entire hotel is centrally heated with good wooden decor fitting for a 5 star luxury. The best part is the welcoming staff and service which greet you each time you pass them. Room service is very quick and so is housekeeping. I had taken an Himalayan package which included breakfast and dinner. The dinner spread though limited was new each day although few of Kashmiri signature dishes would be welcomed. Breakfast was almost same but good enough to start the day if you stay here and want to go out for ski-ing , my main purpose. I had booked a premier room which is the most basic yet it was spacious. The higher suites have balcony but for family of 2 with a kid the premier room is good enough. The hotel is located near the gondola but one needs to climb a steep incline to reach it so take that in mind if one commutes each day for ski-ing. Ski equipment can be rented at hotel. The hotel charges around 4000INR for pickup and drop one way to the airport which is quite steep. One can easily get good cars around 2000-2300 from srinagar or Gulmarg so keep that in mind. Most times the vehicles if snow is too much get changed at tanmarg which charge around 700 rs till gulmarg, still a good bargain. I also used the spa facilities. The treatments range from 3500 [massage per 60minutes]. the whole approach is good and is relaxing and one can opt for the spa after a day long ski. The hotel has a heated pool just to relax. Its small so do not entertain much of swimming but gives great view of the surrounding while resting . The hotel also gives complimentary steam, sauna, jacuzzi facilities along with gym. The view from the room are breathtaking and one can see amazing sunrise and sunset on the peaks To sum up I would say, excellent staff, excellent service, great place and quite a daylight between Khyber and other similar hotels in Gulmarg

tripadvisor · 2015-03-18 · 4/5view source

Loved my stay here ! Loved my stay at the hotel. You can't beat the location. Simply one of the best Hill Resorts in India. New hotel but built in a style that gives its Lobby a Heritage and classy look. A lot of wood work in the hotel. Framed photographs (depicting Kashmiri culture) are used as artwork instead of paintings. Rooms are carpeted, fairly spacious (350 sq ft) and offers stunning views of the pine trees and slopes. I visited during the heaviest snowfall and the views were surreal. The pool area is very good and looks amazing with wild pine trees just behind the glass wall. Play area to keep the kids busy. Wifi is chargeable and so is the mini Theatre. The staff at the hotel is predominantly Kashmiris and this I personally think is highly commendable as employment to locals is the necessary component of Eco Tourism. The check-in at the hotel was smooth and quick. We were given a hot cup of 'Kahwa' in the coffee lounge and by the time we finished our drink, the keys were ready and we were escorted to the room not by a porter but by a smart front office executive himself. Room service is prompt and orders were ready in no time. I waited for only 15 minutes for the Dinner to be served in the room. However the quality of service is not uniform as one would expect from a luxury hotel. The dinner plates were not cleared till my check-out next morning. This is unimaginable for a luxury resort. More training is required for the staff especially at the restaurant. During breakfast, I had to ask for water and tea/coffee. In a Luxury resort and for that matter in any good hotel/resort, it is expected from the waiters to keep a tab on the tables and fill/refill glass of water even before the guests ask for it. I asked for a glass of water and I was given one and still the water was not served to my family which is nothing but a lackadaisical attitude of the service staff. But these are minor glitches and I am sure it will improve with times. Bathrooms are spacious with bathtub, shower cubicle and a transparent window that separates it from the bedroom. If the blinders are up, you can enjoy the view of the pine trees from the bathtub itself. Wooden blinders looks good but messy to operate. If I start nitpicking, I would be tempted to say that the WC shower appears tacky and very low quality. The food is said to be highlight of the hotel. I am a vegetarian and not a foodie so can't comment much on it but have heard very good things about the food at this hotel. One should know that there is NO BAR at the hotel so one must come prepared with his stuff packed. There is a nice Cigar lounge for the smokers. I work for a travel company and overall I find it a very nice resort which I can easily recommend to my clients. I loved my stay in the hotel.

tripadvisor · 2014-07-02 · 5/5view source

Khyber is Gulmarg, Gulmarg is Khyber From where do I start.. Gulmarg is a very small area and not much to do except the Gondola ride (3-4 hours max) but Khyber Resort has turned this idle location into an exceptionally perfect holiday place. The view from the resort entirely engulfs the beauty of Gulmarg. The facilities of the resort are comprehensive - nicely done rooms, fully equipped with modern amenities(airtel HD TV in rooms - Taj does not provide it in Srinagar) , play areas outside and inside for kids(XBox games) , spa for adults, swimming pool, jacuzzi, steam, sauna, mini theatre, tea lounge, hookah bar and lovely sitting areas all along the property. The staff is well trained and a special mention for team leader 'Zahoor'. The space has been used very well to give you a wholesome experience; from majestic entrance, grand lobby to walkways. For e.g. the walk towards the spa/pool area itself is so beautiful that you don't mind taking the many stairs it involves, very thoughtfully sitting areas are made along the walkway. Their Spa is far superior than many in urban cities. Our stay at Taj in Srinagar was disappointing as compared to Khyber Resort. If you are visiting Kashmir do not miss Khyber Resort at any cost.

tripadvisor · 2014-07-02 · 5/5view source

Oasis in Heaven Just a few things you should know before we talk about the hotel. Gulmarg is an incredibly beautiful place especially in the months of Dec-Feb given fresh powder snow, cleanliness, not very crowded, and incredible natural beauty. We would definitely recommend this period. it is also the best time to enjoy Khyber - a rare high end and full service option available in the hilly regions in India. Khyber was less than a 2 hour drive away from Srinagar airport. The roads were good and coupled with the beautiful surroundings - we had a good start to our journey. The hotel greets you with a hot cup of traditional Kashmiri Kahwa which is a must have. I would rank the hotel ambiance in line with the top premium 5 stars in India. The hotel is indeed located in the middle of a field which was covered by heavy snow and the beauty was amplified with several pine trees. For once the hotel looked much more spectacular than even the pictures on the website both from outside and inside. The rooms are tastefully decorated and provide equally outstanding views with no stones unturned for the good experience. The hotel has centralized temperature control and all rooms are fitted with modern amenities. You can go outside, play in the snow for however long you want and the warm hotel awaits you the moment you start freezing. They have lots of designated areas to enjoy the snow, for outside views etc. in case you just with to relax in the hotel. Just one complaint, walls are thin. We unfortunately shared a wall with a noisy group in the other room which was not very helpful in the night. The Gondola is walking distance away from the hotel which gives easy access to the higher points in case you want to ski. We did not try the spa, but spa, heated swimming pool, jaccuzi etc were available. The hospitality is at par with the Oberoi service which I rate at the highest level in this country. We had a small accident while skiing and the hotel staff took great care of us during the rest of our stay. The food quality was good, but the options are a but limited. The hotel has a buffet and a multicuisine ala carte menu. However, you can run out of options during a longer stay. The hotel staff can however help with some off the menu options as well in case you are craving for something else. The hotel has only one restaurant (all day) and one cafe. the restaurant options to me was a limitation. Alcohol is not served in the hotel. The only other shortcoming was our departure from the hotel. The hotel had run out of cars and hence had provided us with a local taxi. We had paid double the money for a hotel car which could directly take us to the airport, instead we had to travel in local ones and change car in between (one for snow and one for road). Overall, we were well taken care of, and without exaggerating, I can safely say that this was a superior experience than some of our other trips to Europe and New Zealand, both in terms of scenic beauty and hospitality (that too at a fraction of the cost).
