tripadvisor · 2014-05-28 · 3/5view source

Luxury without deliverance! I came back last month from an enthralling & rejuvenating holiday with my husband, in Kashmir. It was on our list for quite sometime, so we booked everything including accommodation, transport, tickets & some sightseeing, in advance. Though it was just the beginning of the tourist season, we were witness to traffic jams, queues, stampedes & cacophony all around us! God save those who plan on visiting in the high tourist season(may to july)! Lets talk about the great Khyber Himalayan Resort & Spa, Gulmarg, which claims itself to be the only 5 star property in the whole of Jammu & Kashmir state! Nestled in the Himalayan mountain ranges with striking views of the Affarwat Peaks in Gulmarg, India, it is no doubt a luxury property. It lies 65 kilometres away from Srinagar Airport ( 3 hour drive ) on top of a hill with views to die for(specially in winters)! The resort has eighty rooms, four luxury cottages and a Presidential Suite each with breath-taking views of the Affarwat Peaks and of the valley. Out of the 3 categories of rooms, the premier, premier mountain view & the luxury balcony rooms, we stayed in the luxury room with a grand balcony. There are several dining options like their restaurant called Cloves with splendid views, Nouf a covered deck to enjoy cosy meals besieged by breath-taking sights of the snow covered mountains, Chaikash a tea lounge with myriad flavours of teas to choose from & Calabash the cigar & Sheesha lounge. The Khyber Spa by L’Occitane is one of India’s most luxurious and holistic well-being spas, again with magnificent view. One cannot spend a dull moment in this imposing environment as a whole lot of recreational activities awaits you such as a fully dedicated area for children called the Igloo, a stupendous heated indoor swimming pool, gymnasium, game area for adults so on so forth. But for me everything ceases to importance if one fails to deliver! They might have been recognised for their outstanding service delivery by the World Luxury Hotels, but I doubt it was the opinion of the guest or the staff itself! On this wise, in all humility & complete fairness, I express my analysis of the Khyber Himalayan Resort as follows: 1. There were security screening machines installed at the entrance without people to operate them. My husband & I were not greeted upon arrival nor was there an inspection for security of persons, only for the baggage. I saw used overcoats just lying on the ground at the entry steps without being taken care off or noticed! I asked one of the guards if mine will be treated similarly! When we entered the resort, we were asked to identify the luggage, which my husband did. Then while we were waiting for the room, another person came & asked to identify the luggage, so I went & did so. It was the height of patience when another of the staff asked us a third time to identify the baggage! It shows the lack of communication among the staff! They seriously need training!! 2. The check-in time was 2:00 pm but we arrived an hour prior. Understanding the scenario we calmly waited, had buffet lunch & approached at the reception after an hour. While I was clicking pictures of the entrance I noticed that they are a part of the SLH group so I asked the reservations guy to upgrade my room. This took another 45-50mins & we were escorted by Ms.Shivangi to our room. This waiting for 2 hours killed us & wasted more than half of our day! Nevertheless, we checked in our luxurious room with a balcony & found it super comfortable & welcoming with respect to the freezing cold outside! 3. We took wi-fi for 24 hrs by paying INR.750, which we later realised didn't work for most of the time. Also, the sliding door between the dressing area & bathroom was completely broken! 4. When we went to the restaurant to eat lunch where I met a family from Mumbai who were guests there as well. We immediately struck a conversation & they told me about the menu being repeated everyday. Like the manchow soup was served a day before & was repeated the day after. There was less choice for vegetarians for the outrageous price. Also, I did not find the food to the standard of a luxury class hotel. I went & informed this to Mr.Manzoor, who was at the front office & asked to be spoken to someone responsible. He said he will send someone to speak to me regarding the same, but in vain. I asked twice, but no one bothered to attend to me. In my opinion, he is seriously in need of proper training from a branded corporate organisation. This is absolutely not expected of this kind of a property. If it was Taj or Oberoi....immediate action would have been taken with the staff personally taking responsibility. 5. I usually work on my machine in the night where I require to use the internet. I tried to connect several times but it was futile. Hence I called the reception, who transferred me to the IT department. After explaining the problem, he informed me that he cannot help me as everything seems to be alright at his end! I wonder why they charge for something they cannot deliver! 6. In the morning we looked forward to the breakfast, only to experience a bolt from the blue! It was pathetic! I ordered a masala dosa( a south Indian pancake made from rice batter), which was not even close to be called a dosa....very chewy with bland chutney! Then I tried my hands on parantha( an Indian shallow fried bread), it was hard as a rock! The food was cold, tasteless & disgusting! I again called the staff to make them aware of the situation....but no one was suited to handle the situation. 7. We wanted to enjoy a swim in the inviting pool but as we didn't carry our costumes we couldn't. But I observed a couple of children & adults swimming in the pool without costumes. I asked for explanation but they just cleared the air saying we cannot do anything, as they do not listen to us! That was very convincing!!! 8. I am a traveller & a photographer who want to bring back places, people, destinations, cultures & a profusion of elements to the less travelled, confined at home. So I approached Mr.Manzoor again to let me have a person escort me, show around the property & photograph it. Apparently that didn't happen either. 9. A warning for those taking for granted that this property being a 5 star luxurious one would serve alcohol....let me speak loud & clear, " this property does not have a bar or a liquor license ", so be prepared! 10. Another letdown which came as shock to us later was the situation of transportation once we reached the hotel in Gulmarg. We were not informed of the fact that one cannot move in & around Gulmarg in an vehicle which is registered outside of Gulmarg. Even the hotel people cannot let their vehicle for travel within Gulmarg. So we either need to walk, take an ATV(all terrain vehicle, super expensive!) or hire a local car. 11. This, what I am about to tell is rather unusual. Everytime a member of the staff went pass us in the hotel grounds, they acknowledged the presence of my husband only greeting him, hello sir, good morning sir...blah, blah & NOT ME! I don't understand why? I have noticed this for the first time in my life! Overall, it is a great option to stay in Gulmarg during winters for the location, view, snow, heating & indoor recreational activities, ONLY IF THEY IMPROVE UPON THEIR SERVICE, COMPETENCY, ATTITUDE & STAFF!

BUT even in winter the rooms get very very stuffy and suffocating at night, so much so that we opened the door the first night a bit and then got table fans to circulate air the following nights. We did complain and people did come to fix but I think their system doesnt go below 24 degrees into air conditioning, so dont know if it was just our room, or whether changing rooms would have made a difference? Also goes to show that their insulation so good or the quality of their glass/windows/doors that despite it being below minus outside, still not freezing in the room! Mattresses are also ok, not particularly comfortable and pillows very large, heavy and uncomfortable. THE MOST IMPORTANT thing is no matter what anyone tells you, please get a HIMALAYAN view room versus the GULMARG view!!!!!! The room with the balcony makes no differeence in winter as balcony does not get used so though we had it, was not worth paying the price difference. In summer, probably yes! Room Service too was extremely helpful and willing to finger cut some carrot and cucumber I had carried with me as my 4 year ols has food allergies! Spa is run by L'occitane and the facial I had was good and therapist though fairly new and from nagaland but decent. Jacuzi and indoor pool was also really enjoyed by the kids! Overall the staff goes out of their way to satisfy and are very courteous and polite in a very genuine way and not just how the thai do it because they have to or are supposed to!! All in all would surely go back, just wish they would aircondition the rooms!

tripadvisor · 2013-12-15 · 5/5view source

Surprised beyond expectation! We selected the hotel after having read the reviews on tripadvisor. Being our first trip to Kashmir, we were skeptical of our decision before reaching the hotel. But, all our doubts took a dive just as we reached the hotel entrance. The magnificence of the property spread across the beautiful landscape, surrounded by pine trees and snow capped mountains was awe-inspiring. As soon as we entered the reception, we were greeted by Mr. Maroof. I must mention he is a very fine gentlemen. After welcoming us, he insisted that I let him carry my laptop and then he escorted us to Chaikash (tea lounge). He offered us Kava (very rejuvinating) after which we did the check-in formalities. He made sure that we got comfortable, even before we checked-in to the hotel. After check-in he explained to us in detail the various facilities available at the hotel, escorted us to our room and further explained all available features in the room. The icing on the cake was we were upgraded from a premium to a luxury room, that too with one of the best views of the landscape (at the center of the property). One very rare quality that Mr. Maroof displayed was his communication skills. Whenever interrupted by us he would stop talking immediately and listen very patiently to us. And only continue once we stopped. We stayed there for 3 nights 2 days. The hotel is just 5 minutes walk to the Gondola, which is great, no need to hire a taxi or ponies to reach the gondola. The room was very comfortable with king size beds and very comfortable pillows, mattresses etc. Nothing less than a 5-star property. I am surprised at many reviews with negative comments for their food. I must mention we had at least 4 meals (vegetarian) at the hotel (apart from breakfast) and the food was definitely very good. And I am surprised to read about the negative comments. They have a varied selection of cuisine on their menu, including Chinese, Italian and North Indian. Although the options for vegetarians was limited to few items, the food was very good. A must try is thier Mezze Platter (falafel with pita, tatzaki, hummous etc). I was absolutely delighted with the flavours of Arabia in midst of Kashmir!! Even the vegetarian wrap is worth a try. One wrap is quite filling for 2 people, after you have had soup. The servings portions are decent size; one portion is good for 3-4 people. The chaikash (tea lounge) has over 25 varieties of tea, while the Kava is complimentary. Another gesture by the hotel was a complimentary car, on one of the nights when we wanted to go out at eat. Just for a change, we had dinner at a nearby Bakshi restaurant. The food was decent. Although it was 75% cheaper than Khyber, it would be injustice to compare it to Khyber. If anything negative about the food at khyber, then its the price. Although expensive, it is definitely worth the price. You get what you pay for. Another gesture worth mentioning is that, we got a call from the manager at Cloves (the restaurant at Khyber), on the morning we were checking out. We were late for breakfast so he called us to ensure that we would not miss the breakfast. I don't think you would get such personal care at a 5 or 7-star property. I did not want to leave and had to force myself to leave the care & comfort of the hotel located at one of the best landscapes in the world. I will definitely be back at Khyber.

tripadvisor · 2013-10-03 · 3/5view source

Bitter sweet experience at Khyber We stayed at the Khyber Resort at Gulmarg along with four friends on the night of 29th September. The weather was perfect with just a little chill as we drove into Gulmarg. The property is strikingly beautiful to say the least and is built like a fortress cocooning you in a world of beauty, peace and tranquility. It is unlike most properties that one typically sees at the hill stations- it is very luxurious and tastefully done. The lobby, the tea lounge, the restaurants every thing is very warm and comfortable. The food is quite nice with a fair amount of variety for the breakfast and dinner buffet. The rooms are spacious, very well appointed and comfortable. The staff is courteous and helpful. The only problem- but that is a big one is the lack of functioning elevators at a '7 star' place which is built over multiple levels in separate blocks that are not inter connected. We were in rooms two levels below the lobby level and each floor had 3 flights of stairs. In spite of the place having a soft launch over a year ago; the lack of a spa,proper gym etc. may not matter but the absence of elevators at a place more than 8500 feet above sea level is a serious issue. In fact, it made us curtail our vacation plan by a day due to the effect of the numerous steps augmenting the high altitude effect. We found Khyber as one of the most amazing properties at a hill station in India. The presence of functioning elevators will go a long way in elevating the guest experience further.

Good property but Improvements needed! We were a group of 13 adults and 3 children. We stayed at this property for 2 nights (Check in 15th Aug and Check out 17th Aug). Before our stay, I did write to Mr Alex (G.M.) about looking forward to a great stay. He was prompt in his reply and assured us of a good stay. Even Mr Arun (Director of food and beverages) got in touch with me to enquire if we had any special dietary requirements. A good start!! Our stay at this hotel could clearly be divided into two different experiences. To begin with we arrived at the hotel at 4.30pm.We were allotted 3 luxury balcony rooms (east wing) on the first floor and 3 premier mountain rooms on the minus one level (This was after requesting for all 6 rooms on the lobby level as we had senior citizens and children in our group and the hotel lift is still not operational). The premier mountain view rooms are around 386 sq. feet. The rooms were very modern, well maintained with flat screen television and tea/coffee warm kettle, centrally air conditioned, etc. Similarly the bathroom was fantastic with a nice shower section and a bath tub. But on close inspection the bath tub had brown dirt stains which was a downer. The phone and television was not working!!! I had to personally walk to the reception to inform about the problems and they said they would have it rectified. The view from the mountain view rooms was an absolute disappointment. On opening the curtains all we could see is trees and the parking complex!!! In the defense of the hotel management we faced cloudy and rainy weather during our stay in Gulmarg which had curtailed the views to an extent. By dinner time, nobody had yet come to rectify the above issues. I,my wife and my 6 month old daughter went for dinner and this time we were clear about our disappointment. Fortunately, we ran into Mr Arun and Mr Deepak who understood our concerns and promptly changed our room to a luxury balcony room (east wing) on the lobby level. From then on, our stay only got better. The luxury balcony rooms (568 sq. feet) are absolute luxury. Huge rooms and bathrooms with a wonderful balcony overlooking the valley. If there are no budget constraints I would definitely advice people to take balcony rooms but in the south wing. The east wing rooms have a good view but the view from the south wing is definitely better. I happened to meet Mr Alex the next day. He explained that since the last 3 days it was raining heavily in Gulmarg hence they were facing issues with their telephone and televisions. He acknowledged the problem of the bath tub which he said was a problem in their system which he was going to completely overhaul! The breakfast spread in the hotel was great. South India, Pav Bhaji, cereals, milk shakes, juices, sausages, baked beans, cappuchino, kahva etc... Our first night dinner at Cloves restaurant was average. They have a buffet (Rs 1600 p.h.) but we opted for ala carte. Most of the dishes are priced in the range of Rs 600-700 plus tax. Our next days dinner at Nouf (open only for Dinner) was great. Its an open air grill restaurant and definitely pricier (Rs 800-Rs 1000 per dish plus tax) but the food was fantastic. At Nouf we can bring our own alcohol and they are happy to serve it to us. This restaurant has limited capacity and tends to go full. Hence prior reservations recommended. Vodafone postpaid subscribers will not get network in the hotel except in the balcony of the luxury balcony rooms. There is full network when u exit the hotel. The hotel also has a cigar and sheesha lounge. The hotel does laundry for children clothes at 50 % of the normal laundry tariff. Gulmarg does not have wine shops. No hotel in Gulmarg serves alcohol. The spa and swimming pool are not operational. They have a private theatre which unfortunately was booked during our stay. They have a small children's activity play area. Mr Alex and his team have a great property. I am sure they will iron out the glitches faced by us. I would recommend a stay at this property when all their facilities are operational (January 2014 onwards).

tripadvisor · 2013-07-19 · 4/5view source

Unexpected Luxury nestled in Himalayas... Gulmarg tends to be touristy and crammed during summers but Khyber’s location ensures that you’re far and away from the madding crowds besides offering you with some of the best views of the layered mountains and the mist. This is one place that will overwhelm you with its sheer grand feel, as you seldom come across a luxurious resort of this size in the hill stations. Mostly, we expect a simple & functional lobby and basic room standards, but a few addresses like this have truly raised the bar. Don’t miss the traditional Kashmiri Kahwa on arrival while their staff completes the check-in formalities. It’s very intimate and comfortable lounge at the far end of the extended lobby, with large bay windows separating sunlit verandahs overlooking the hills. The rooms have the typical comforts of five-star hotel and resorts in other parts of the country, just that you would not have expected it here. The décor is creatively inspired by local Kashmiri artistic motifs. However, despite being a new hotel, some structural elements had maintenance issues like the sliding door between the wardrobe and toilet. We had a large spacious balcony sit out attached to our room, a perfect place to brood and admire the blissful views. With Kashmir having extended daylight in summers, you have the flexibility to optimize your hours between the balcony and bathtub as the view gets replicated from toilet as well. We preferred taking all our meals at the resort itself. The breakfast and weekend dinner buffet spread was extensive and a-la-carte menu had some interesting local cuisine choices. Service was a need area at times, as the staff was not well-trained to be attentive or make eye contacts. They looked busy all the time without really attending to the guests. Service issue was also faced when we had to request thrice for something as simple as keeping an extra duvet in the room. Otherwise, the team is very knowledgeable to interact with and seemed to have background experiences with some of the leading International hotel brands. The resort management also went out of their way to arrange Gondola tickets for us, a must-do experience if you are in Gulmarg. This saved us from the frustration of getting into long queues. We were advised to come back in winters, esp. February to experience another aspect of Gulmarg. Khyber is a destination in itself and you’re tempted to be back here soon, hopefully, their spa would be operational soon.

tripadvisor · 2013-07-16 · 4/5view source

Excellent service & well appointed rooms Possibly the best hotel in Gulmarg, the Khyber is an excellent, brand new property. The hotel has the feel of a "wood cabin" with great views of the mountains & woods. Pros: 1. Located very close to the Gondola starting point & the golf course. 2. Service is outstanding with eager to please staff. 3. Rooms are brand new and were the most comfortable in Kashmir. 4. Food though limited in choice, was generally decent. 5. Chaikash their tea lounge is a great hang out and serves lovely kawa. Also decent cappuccino. 6. The room we had, overlooked the hotel gardens which are perfect to relax in. Hotel also does bonfires on request for an additional charge. 7. Kids have a cinema, trampoline, billiards table (poorly maintained), garden area to play in, all of which keeps them busy. 8. We shared our suite with 2 kids, which worked out well. The room balcony was spectacular. Cons: 1. The hotel has some construction still underway. Can be noisy if you are nearer the construction. 2. The approach to the hotel is cluttered with ponies which make a real mess. No real fault of the hotel, Gulmarg is just like that. 3. The hotel restaurant was shut which meant that you had a choice between the buffet & an open air continental restaurant. The buffet tries hard, but isn't very good. It also gets boring if you are going to eat three meals times 3 days in the same room! Gulmarg has no other real options outside the hotel. 4. The lack of authentic Kashmiri food was our greatest disappointment. When in Kashmir why would you want to eat continental? Perhaps for the europeans who come skiing in the winter! 5. NO ALCOHOL...... so BYOB! Overall, I would highly recommend Khyber.

tripadvisor · 2013-06-10 · 4/5view source

Excellent Location, Limited options but a greedy management ! this property being a member of the SLH group boasts of excellent infrastructure, like other SLH properties. however there are some major flaws too ! we stayed for 3 days in end of may 2013 in balcony room no. 2019 - first floor. the property is located slightly above the Gondola boarding station gate which is past the taxi stand with horses blocking the main road leading to the property. Secondly the most annoying factor is that the management has installed mobile phone jammers high up on a pine tree which works by blocking all mobile phones & wifi above the lobby level; in spite of full signal one cannot make or receive calls. This has been done so that the guests staying in the property use the room telephone & pay the inflated long distance charges. The mobile phone operators whose fake full signal one will receive when within the hotel are Aircel, Airtel & Cellone (BSNL POSTPAID). The staff are also over worked & work beyond their normal shift hours & with no lift in the property, it is really physically challenging for them too especially in peak season ! The spa & swimming pool are not yet ready but noise levels for the on-going construction activity during day time is low, hence that's tolerable. the f&b options for veg & non-veg are limited & highly priced, however the food is tasty. waffle counter missing in the breakfast hall & buffet lunch & dinner are prices @ 1600/- per person but with a limited spread. the kava served in the lobby is excellent but not the original Kashmiri kava as its without tea leaves ! the room temperature cannot be increased beyond 24 deg C and that's a problem for kids who would like to sleep at 27 deg C, the most comfortable temperature. in-room digital temperature controller though is of high quality and at par with other SLH properties like Wild Flower Hall of Oberoi above Shimla ! Presently the best in Gulmarg, hence full occupancy.. and since its a new property the minor issues should hopefully be sorted out soon ! Getting your own vehicle & keeping it with you will help as car hire options are limited too !

tripadvisor · 2013-04-05 · 5/5view source

In the lap of the gods It's a completely contemporary building, but made of wood and stone, with a few elements of local Kashmiri architecture. There's nothing like it in the rest of the Kashmir Valley, and it is clear that it has been made with passion and inspiration. There is a wealth of handicrafts all over the building, but taken together, they don't give the look of a junky handicraft store. The food is superb, whether it is the Irish Angus steak (I asked for mine blue and it came perfectly done) to the Kashmiri banquet dishes; from the Illy coffee in the lounge to the ginger cookies that are served with kahwa, fragrant with saffron. This is easily the best resort/hotel/guest house in the Kashmir Valley. And further even. The warmth of the mainly Kashmiri staff and their professionalism will have me going back for more!

finally gulmarg gets what it deserves! at the outset i would like to confess that we did not stay here - came to check out the property because we were looking for an alternative to our hotel we were unhappy at. it is located at a height, close to the gondola ski lift where the serious skiers would want to be. since the property is very new, they do not have protocols set in place for skiers - people were entering with their ski boots and ski sticks - which must be murder on the flooring as well as carpeting! they need to set up a changing room/ lounge for skiers to change their footwear etc. The central atrium is awesome with a high dome, wooden flooring, thick carpets and walls adorned with great paintings. we sat in the tea lounge and ordered the kashmiri tea - ' kahwa' and it was delicious - flavoured with saffron and served with sliced almonds on the side. had a chat with the hotel manager who was knowledgeable, pleasant, helpful and passionate about his role - the plan is to set up a spa as well as a year round swimming pool soon. he proudly announced that the property is already a member of the 'small luxury hotel' group - a great honour. we dined at the coffee shop - the menu is extensive, compared to what was till now available at Gulmarg. The order got confused - they got us stuff we had not ordered, but were prompt to rectify. a great property, very very new, hopefully with a great future!

Chad B. (1/5) · Online reviews are a tricky beast. With all the AI out there it is quite difficult to discern if you are reading a real experience from a real human. This I can assure you is genuine and mainly intended as a word of caution so no one else has to suffer the experience my wife and I endured at the Phoenician in September of 2023. This review has nothing to do with the room quality or amenities or facilities, those are all quite nice and frankly should be for the nightly rate you are paying. This review ties directly to the staff and their lack of care for their guests. We were there for a weekend with family and friends attending a wedding. On the day of the wedding my wife went to the pool for one hour by herself. She got her towels and set her things down on a chair. She then received a phone call that she took and spoke for 15 minutes next to the pool with her back turned to the chair. At this time someone grabbed all of her things including her purse and sandals. When she turned back around and realized her things were gone she immediately alerted the staff including waitresses and the towel clerk who had been working the area. This pool probably had 20 guests at the pool at this time, not a lot of activity. All the staff had the same lethargic response, "I am sorry maam, there is nothing we can do." Now let me be crystal clear, we are not ascribing blame directly to the hotel. They certainly cannot be responsible for all guests' belongings at all times and my wife made a costly error in judgment, although an unconscious one, she never should have turned her back to her items. We immediately shut down credit cards but saw that the culprit already tried to charge $2K+ at Neiman Marcus just down the street, so now we had validation that this was no accident or misplaced items. There was one gentleman that worked the hotel security, I believe his name was Thomas, and he was the only one that took any ownership of the issue and showed an ounce of empathy towards our predicament. The extent of effort from the rest of management at the Phoenician was to blink, stand still and continue to say "I am sorry, there is nothing we can do." One gentleman who worked at the front desk (I wish I had his name) even had the nerve to tell us, "Things get stolen here all the time" as if they had as much control over this as the weather. Further, if that statement is true, maybe an examination of security protocols might be a priority. Rather than attend our wedding we spent the reception filling out a police report. We knew the chances of recovery were slim and the financial loss was significant ($500+) but not catastrophic. I can hear the critics whispering, "first world problems." The point is there is an absolute dearth of security at this resort. I can't list all the shortcomings but here are a few: There are no cameras anywhere on the three pool decks (at least that is what we were told), All gates to the pools remain wide open at all times. I have stayed at resorts who charge half as much as the phonecian and they still require a room key to access the pools. The parking lot entry attendant showed no enforcement as far as which cars were allowed on property. We were waived through, never asked for a name or room number. If I was a thief I couldn't ask for a more vulnerable environment. Here is the kicker. 7 hours after the purse was first stolen another charge was attempted on our canceled credit card BACK ON THE PHOENICIAN property. We thought this was our chance, how many establishments are even open at 10 PM to accept payment on the property? Less than five we thought. We alerted hotel management immediately and again the blank stares, the nonchalance, complete and utter deflection, "I am sorry, we can't track that." How easy would it have been upon our initial claim to tell their staff "If you run a credit card that is denied then alert security immediately." With today's tech and ubiquitous communication that is such an easy catch. You would think at $700/night there might be a modicum of effort or assistance for their guests. We were routed to some off-sight Marriott risk assessor that did not respond to us for three weeks only to tell us "our investigation results in no compensation to the guest." I can only imagine the depths they went into that investigation. Moral of the Story: Don't take anything valuable to the pool Second Moral of the Story: Don't rely on the staff. Their customer service extends as far as reading a script, over and over again. They are sorry, but there is nothing they can do, ahem, willing to do.

(5/5) · I've stayed at several Starwood properties in the greater Phoenix area (Westin Kierland Villas, W Scottsdale, Four Points Tempe) and the overall experience at the Phoenician tops them all. The service was top notch, breakfasts on site were great, and we had a blast at the pools. The overall décor seemed a bit dated (or at least not my stle), but that didn't distract from the overall experience. LOCATION: Located less than 10 minutes from Old Town Scottsdale and Scottsdale Fashion Square (shopping, eating, and drinking), the Phoenician is a great home base for a trip to the desert. If you're in town for Spring Training, the Phoenician offers free shuttle service to the Giants games with Salt River Fields (D'Backs and Rockies) less than 20 minutes away and a host of other teams within a reasonable drive. ROOM: We stayed in Casita "A" in Room 7103 for three nights. We were on the ground floor and our sliding glass doors opened up onto a patio overlooking the large lawn and the Necklace Lake. We had a king bed and a rollaway bed for aC. The bathroom was spacious with a separate shower and tub. There was a small nook in the hall area with a coffee maker, waters, etc. Even with the addition of the rollaway bed, there was plenty of space for two adults and child to move around, hang out, and not feel cramped. It was a great setup for a family as aC could walk right out to play on the lawn and we were a very short walk from the various pools. If you have kids, I'd opt for a ground floor Casita room rather than an upstairs unit. Parking was available right outside the Casita, which made getting around a breeze. I didn't feel like we missed out on anything by not being in the main building. RESORT: First of all, the service from the front desk to the pool to the restaurant was fantastic every step of the way. Everyone was friendly, attentive, and made us feel welcome. This is the kind of service every hotel should have. We spent a lot of time at the gorgeous pools and on the waterslide, and aC joined the other kids on the property running back and forth trying not to fall off the floating mats (which I like to think of as "Wipeout Jr."). The Surge splash area hadn't yet opened, but we could tell that it would've been a great hangout for little kids. The only possible thing that was missing was a lazy river (like at the Westin Kierland) but there was still plenty to do at the pools here. Finding chairs to lounge in was not an issue. While we never dropped aC off the Funician Kids Club, we did take her to Hoppy Hour where she was able to pick up a stuffed animal bunny, which is a great souvenir and reminder of our time at the Phoenician. SPG PLATINUM: This was my 50th stay (not nights but stays) at a Starwood property, and we were welcomed to our room by a cheese plate, bottles of Voss water, a Phoenician beach/pool bag, pool toys, and goggles. Given that this was a family trip, they couldn't have chosen a better way to greet us and aC was very excited about all the "presents." We didn't have to pay a resort fee or for anything other than the drinks we ordered at the pool. We had the full run of the breakfast buffet (pastries, fruit, meats, smoothies, omelet bar, etc.) every morning and we were never once charged (contrast with some Starwoods where you only get a continental breakfast).

Veronica P. (4/5) · The Phoenician, a Luxury Collection Hotel in the Starwood family, is somewhat oddly located in the Scottsdale area. As you enter the property, you will encounter a guard gate. You will merely be asked if you are checking into the hotel, there for dinner, to play golf, etc. and pointed towards the appropriate parking area. The Phoenician offers valet parking, of course, or complimentary covered self-parking. If entering through the self-parking garage, you will take the elevator or escalator to the third floor in order to check-in. Check-in was smooth, and all SPG Gold amenities were explained. Since there is a resort fee of $29 per day, which includes internet, I chose the extra 250 points as my amenity. Although the hotel was probably not more than 30% occupied, I did not get an upgrade. I was able to book my room by finding a Better Rate Guarantee, which netted me an extra 2,000 Starpoints so all was not lost. Rooms at The Phoenician are fairly large by most standards, measuring approximately 600 square feet. The Grand Deluxe King Room overlooks the pool areas. There is a balcony with patio furniture, but when its nearly 110 degrees sitting outside doesn't sound like an appealing proposition. Upon entering the room, there is a small bar area with a Keurig coffee maker to the right. The bathroom is located to the left, and is very spacious. There is a large soaking tub, shower and separate toilet. There are double sinks with ample counter space, however three large vases take up some valuable counter space, although it is located over the tub. There are Laboratoire Remede branded bath amenities which have a menthol/minty scent. The bedroom is very spacious with ample room to roam around without running into furniture. The bed is very comfortable, with super soft pillows. There is a bed bench at the foot of the bed. There is a large sofa and lounge chair with coffee table in which to unwind, relax and watch TV or have a conversation. The desk area although fairly large, was lacking in outlets that were convenient to plug in your computer or to charge your phone or gadgets. The Phoenician boasts 9 swimming pools, 27 holes of golf and tennis courts, in addition to a somewhat small gym and full spa. Although a Five Diamond AAA hotel, service was professional but somewhat impersonal. All employees were polite but not overly friendly. The hotel also boasts J&G Steakhouse, a Jean George Vongerichten restaurant. Although I did not get a chance to eat here on this visit, it is definitely on my list. If anything close to my past visit at Prime at the Bellagio this is a sure hit. The property is also starting to show its age, and could use an update in the near future. I would definitely stay here again if I can find a good rate, otherwise there are other properties in the Scottsdale area worth checking out.

If you stay at the resort, I highly recommend participating in the resort events and activities. As a non-yoga person, yoga was surprisingly one of the highlights of my stay. Susanna, the yoga instructor is amazing and truly teaches on every level--including altering her class so that I could participate (full belly and all). My husband enjoyed the cocktail lesson and we loved the live music at the Thirsty Camel at night (see my separate review on Yelp). The bottom line of my review: Would we go back? Absolutely!!!

Bunny G. (5/5) · I fell in love here, I've been a guest here, I've worked here, I met Gregory HInes here, I even served Walter Cronkite a drink here! Walking into the Phoenician is like walking back in time for me. Having worked here when the doors first opened until it was dubbed Club Fed, I've seen the Phoenician evolve from a family-owned business to a corporate-owned resort which at that point lost its luster. Originally you could find fine art hanging on the walls, European decor and the fine bone china used for afternoon tea was the same pattern Princess Diana had in her collection. When you walk in the lobby from the valet, stop in front of the fountain where the star-shaped pattern is on the marble lobby floor. You'll see a gold circle in the middle, that used to be engraved with Mary Elaine's signature. (C.Keating's wife original owner). Just some trivia. Have a cocktail at the Thirsty Camel and sit outside on the balcony. In the evenings they have a piano player so you can listen to live music while you take in the panoramic views of the city. They have fire pits outside and plenty of seating. If you walk down the stairs off the lobby you'll see a wooden door with a stained glass window. That's a private room called the Praying Monk, its a wine cellar that's used for private parties-less than 20 people, it's really cool so check it out on the way to the pool. If you can stay for lunch go to the Terrace and sit on the patio. Order the Prickly Pear Ice-Tea. I also recommend Afternoon Tea served in the lobby. I've had high tea several times here and it's on my top ten list. Chicken salad tea sandwiches are the best. (My top place for high tea? The Peninsula in Hong Kong). Rooms are luxurious, so if you stay, enjoy all the resort has to offer. The spa is fabulous. I've had some treatments here and it's all first class. Schedule some time with a golf pro too, I was lucky I knew one of the pro's and got some lessons myself. If you're up early you can see Coyotes run across the grounds which are cool. During the time I was there, Alessandro Stratta was chef at the French restaurant. It was a fabulous experience-it's now a steak house so I can only say that the location of the restaurant is on the top level so at night views are amazing. Guests in the Presidential Suite ranged from Michael Jackson, Stevie Nicks and Bon Jovi to a Saudi Prince who one night requested Maine Lobster to be flown in from...wait for it...you guessed it! Maine. Having worked in a 5-Star first class hotel, you learn very quickly what true 5-star standards are, so when you travel you're pretty good at deciphering what meets true hospitality standards and the Phoenician still has it all!

Gina T. (4/5) · ** This review is for the spa and pool area only ** I went with a couple of girlfriends on a Saturday to The Phoenician to take advantage of a deal we picked up for a massage, glass of champagne and access to the pool and spa amenities for the day. Overall, the experience was very pleasant. I arrived early for our massage check-in time, since I wanted to try out their gym and steam room. I had no problem with check-in at the spa -- Emily, the girl at the front desk, was very polite and took the time to show me where everything was since I hadn't been there before. The ladies' locker room is nice and very clean -- they do provide you with rubber "slippers" and very heavy white robes to wear for lounging. The gym is not extremely big, but it's a good size compared to other resort gyms I've used and has a number of treadmills, elliptical machines and bikes, along with machines and free weights. After working out, I hit the steam room. The room itself seems a little old and tired, with some grout "issues" and rusty sprinkler heads in the ceiling (however, I didn't find it gross or dirty). It smelled *heavenly,* like eucalyptus. After a steam for about 10 minutes (the steam room wasn't overly hot, which was nice since I could actually relax), I took a shower to get cleaned up for my massage. Their showers are big, with a separate little "staging area" inside them where you can change, dry off, hang your towel/robe and leave your slippers. They do provide you with shampoo, conditioner and body wash, which was high quality and smelled great. My friends and I met up in the waiting area, which is behind the reception desk. Their "water bar" is a nice touch, with several different fruit-flavor-infused waters and hot tea. We waited for maybe 10 minutes or so before our therapists arrived and introduced themselves, leading us back to our separate treatment rooms. My therapist was Mandy and she was fantastic. I am not always a fan of massages because your level of enjoyment can depend on the skill and... shall I say, "chattiness" of the therapist. Mandy was lovely -- she was very sweet and gave a great Swedish-style massage. I am all about NOT TALKING when I get a massage, since I find it much more relaxing to just let my mind shut down and enjoy the pampering. Mandy asked me a couple of questions during the massage (i.e., making sure the pressure was OK and if the room temperature was comfortable) and that was it. I would highly recommend her -- she knows what she's doing and was very professional. I nearly fell asleep a couple of times and I NEVER do that! After we were done, Mandy led me back to the waiting area where I met my friends and we compared notes. Both friends enjoyed their massages, but one did say that her therapist talked the ENTIRE time. We went up to the pool area after freshening up and changing -- the pool area is above the spa, so you walk out the front doors, and then turn around and go the opposite direction. Being a Saturday, the pools were pretty busy, so it took us a little time to find three lounges together. A server found us fairly quickly, and he almost immediately brought us a pitcher of ice water, cups and a menu. The service in the pool area was great -- they were busy, but our server didn't forget about us and was very friendly. I tried their chips, salsa, guacamole and their chicken wrap, which was REALLY good. I really had only two concerns: they were doing some remodeling on one of the pool areas just below us and the fumes were horrible. As in, the fumes gave all three of us headaches and at one point, we were breathing through towels. We mentioned it to our server, who said they were aware of the smell... but there wasn't anything that could be done about it. Had we been able to move to a different location at the pool, we would have -- it was pretty disconcerting. Too bad they were working on it during the middle of a busy Saturday. The other thing was that they had a guy who played steel drums at the pool area. Loudly. All day. Really, I don't mind steel drums. The fellow was talented and having a great time. But for the love of all that's holy, it wasn't fun or relaxing to shout to talk over a guy playing "Billie Jean" on his steel drums... and he was ALL the way on the OTHER SIDE of the pool from us. Had he turned it down a few notches, it would have been fine. Once he wrapped it up for the day, we were able to hear the waterfall, the birds chirping... and not have to yell at each other. No issues with splitting our check for the food and drinks at the pool, either, which was nice. Around 5, we went back to the spa for a quick whirlpool, shower and... we were done! I really enjoyed my time at The Phoenician. Other than the pool remodeling fumes and the excessively loud steel drums, I had a wonderful day and will certainly be back (particularly once the pool remodeling is over!).

The area they serve the tea in is incredibly elegant and outright gorgeous, big fluffy velvet couches, some high backed upholstered chairs, silver place setting and English bone china. They also have a pianist playing calming music that is just loud enough to remind you that he's there. The food and fair is plentiful, the tea is divine and the champagne flowed! Best of all the staff is comforting and they allow you to take your time and enjoy the ritual of the experience. I highly recommend this place! A+!

Adam B. (5/5) · Everything it should be, at summer rates, anyway. Stayed here three nights over Labor Day Weekend on a AAA deal at $167 per night, with a Camelback view. Everything at the resort is on a grand scale, yet not overwhelming, with the exception of it being a bit of a maze to get to some rooms. The lobby has a real wow factor, from the large fountain to the high-ceilinged sitting area with a terrace opening off it... spectacular. I'm something of a hotel bar junkie, so that lobby lounge terrace overlooking the entire Valley of the Sun really impressed me. Rooms are large and supremely comfortable and soothing. Lots of tans and sage greens, with some additional color provided by striking desert botanical prints on the walls. There's plenty of storage/closet/shelf space and a very large-screen TV. Balconies are also very sizeable with a full-length chaise, plus table and chairs. The only complaint I could think of in the rooms is that the nice oval soaking tubs are quite shallow. I would think you would want to carefully consider view at this resort, as there wasn't a great deal of difference in price for room categories, but there was a great deal of difference in view quality. I had the Camelback/cactus garden view on the rear of the hotel, which was quite nice and panoramic. There's also the view of the Valley, same as the lobby terrace--these rooms also overlook the pool complex. But there would be some rooms with views of the entranceway or a rooftop, so if view is important to you, specify what you want. The pool area is spectacular, with waterfalls and lots of angles and cubbyholes, as well as large open swimming areas. Set off to one side was a small courtyard fountain pool with beautiful tiling. It was usually not at all crowded, so if all you wanted to do was lounge in the water, you might try there if there was too much activity at the main pool. I have to give a special nod to the service at the Oasis pool bar--some really good sandwiches and wonderful servers. If the drinks "come out a little big," then you get the extra. The Centre for Well-Being spa, despite the pretentious name, offers great massages and an impressive relaxation area with sleep-inducing zero-gravity chairs. I'm not much on golf or tennis, but I did hear lots of guests praising those facilities. I do enjoy hiking, and it's about a fifteen minute walk on surface streets to get to the trailhead for Camelback Mountain. This hike is moderately difficult, but definitely worth the effort for the views. You get spectacular panoramas of the valley, including the resort. The best views are the first forty-five minutes or so of the trail, so you can make a round trip from your room in about two hours. Two of the primary restaurants now are Windows on the Green, at the golf clubhouse; and Il Terrazzo, in the main building [haven't made it to the new steakhouse yet]. Both are solid, if not spectacular, and offer a wide variety of dishes. I can recommend the gnocchi with wild boar ragout at Il Terrazzo and the quail at Windows. There are a few more casual, cheaper places sprinkled around. Room service serves the Il Terrazzo menu and was very speedy both times I used it. There wasn't a single service mis-step in my three days. Everyone was unfailingly pleasant, efficient, and knowledgeable. Housekeeping came and went without ever interrupting, so there seems to be a real effort to be inconspicuous about servicing the rooms. Some other quick notes: * The ExecuCar service is handy and costs no more than a cab, so should you need to leave the property it's there. * Ice is delivered by room service, but there are also ice machines in the housekeeping closets, which aren't locked. * Lon's at the Hermosa is worth the trip for dinner outside the resort I'm already planning my trip for next 4th of July so I can see the fireworks around the Valley from the lounge terrace.
